This is the live screen fight the stub still does not reproduce, and it is now the second live match lost at exactly the parity floor (match 13 was the first, 12:1). **The floor was tested and left alone.** Raising `PARITY_FLOOR` from 0.97 to 1.0 — never take a flag that leaves us weaker — makes the army far stronger (81 scenarios better, kills twice as fast: m9 hunter 381 -> 154, m9 nine 268 -> 147, sleeper grinds 658 -> 397) but costs the points half (58 worse, failures 5 -> 13), and one result settles it: on the `none` maps, where the enemy takes no flag either, the match ends **0:0 after 2000 ticks** — with the enemy at full power, every flag makes us the weaker side, so a floor of 1.0 forbids scoring at all, for ever. The floor must be under one; 0.97 is one flag's worth of debuff, and the doctrine it serves stands. What is open is the fight at that ratio: an even fight should trade evenly, and this one traded three of our melee for their ranged.

**Match 28 (05.09.2026, the first match of v28's next series and the one it lost, rating 1029 -> 1022; player 1 from (12,9)):** lost on points 12400:23699 while holding **eleven creeps at full health against five** and twice their power (3479 against 1726) — the clearest statement yet that this bot's remaining defect is not the fight but the chase. The hunt itself worked: the enemy went from fourteen creeps to six by t=210, and from there we were three times its strength. **Then the army spent 1300 ticks chasing the remnant across the map and killed exactly one of them (t=790), while the points deficit grew from 2925 to 11299.** From t=920 to the end the enemy's hit total never moved off 7200/7200 — not one net point of damage in 580 ticks — although the gap between the centroids fell to three cells again and again ((38,55) against (39,66) at t=1090, (65,31) against (66,34) at t=1260): they kite at our own speed, and a chase at equal speed never ends. **Neither stall net can see this by construction, because both measure geometry and the failure is in the product:** the picket needs armed prey inside `ENGAGE_RANGE` for `STALL_TICKS` consecutive ticks (they leave, and the counter resets) and caps the picket at `STALL_PICKET` = 4 *catchable* enemies (all five of these were catchable), while the march stall needs the armed centroid frozen for `MARCH_STALL_TICKS` (the march moved perfectly well — it just moved nowhere useful). The opening cost the other 2925: the parity gate held the army at HOLD from t=50 to t=90 while the enemy took all seven flags, which is the doctrine working as designed — their power collapsed to 2954 against our 4179 — but it hands over a lead that has to be won back later. **The map is now `map-match28.txt`, and the `farm` script on it reproduces the defeat directly (14345:23358), as `spread` and `sleeper` do.** **Ten eliminations were tried and every one was rejected by the stub, in this order.** *A third stall net keyed on the army's hit total* — ticks since the enemy's total last made a new low, patience 150: 9 scenarios worse, 3 better, and m25 army went from the enemy army destroyed at 396 to **our** army lost at 648, because the clock ran from tick 1 and cut the first legitimate approach march. *The same with a "reached them and took nothing" gate*: 7 worse, m21 army destroyed@416 -> LOST@777 — the net fired at t=290, exactly as the enemy army arrived, and a stall also silences `contactFight`. *Per-creep kiter memory inside `catchable`, patience 150*: 33 worse, 11 better — the whole sleeper/block/wing line fell from annihilation to a points win, because a creep healed back on the spot counts as "no progress" too. *Plus a "he has fled since" condition*: 20 worse, 7 better, m22 army -> LOST@628. *The same at patience 300* was the best of them and still not good enough: the landing gate stayed green, the total margin over all 182 scenarios rose by 32 635 and m21 farm turned from a loss into a win, but failures went 5 -> 6 (m20 and m25 spread from a points win to a points loss) and annihilations 113 -> 111. *At 450*: failures 5 -> 7. *At 350*: failures 5 -> 8, and it breaks the gate itself (m18 farm and m20 farm from win to loss). **That last pair is what settles it: the outcome flips on fifty ticks of the constant, so the threshold is a fit to 182 scenarios rather than a rule, and a named number this sensitive is exactly the one that becomes a lie against the next opponent.** *Splitting `prey` (hunt, intercept and dash) from `catchable` (local fire)*: failures 7, m20 farm from win to loss. *The same through a separate `reachable` used only by the local engage*: failures 7, same gate break — allowing local fire on a kiter simply recreates the chase one creep at a time. *Letting the kiter label expire after `MARCH_STALL_TICKS`*: failures 6, but m18 farm and m20 farm from win to loss. Everything is reverted; **the fruitless chase is the open finding, and it now has a stub scenario of its own on its own map.** Result: lost 12400:23699 at t=1500 with 11 creeps against 5 and 14800/14800 hits, army never engaged after t=790.

**Match 29 (05.09.2026, v28's third series — three wins in 300, 400 and 1100 ticks, then the fourth lost, rating 1034 -> 1022; player 2 from (85,88), against the opponent the second match of the series had annihilated at 4110 against 2368):** the army was destroyed by t=200 in an **even fight** — the second live match lost exactly at parity (match 27 was the first), and this time the log shows the whole mechanism. We took R3 at t=41 (`R×0.8`), they took D5 at t=46 (`D×1.1`, they take more) and came straight at us: contact at t=67 by (78,60) at 3967 against 3896, retreat not feasible. Our four melee died one at a time — t=104, 143, 167, 198 — and until our army was gone the enemy lost **one scout and no combat creep**; from t=210 our power reads 0 (four ranged at `R×0.8` = 192 against their 216 of triple heal — Lanchester's zero), the last healer lived to t=600, and they collected H4, A3, A3 and R3 at 405–568 behind it. **The geometry at t=100 says why.** Their fighting eight stood in a 4×3 block at (70–73, 63–65) — a front row `R-M-R` at x=73 with the three healers *adjacent* at x=72 — and never moved; the other six were thirteen cells away. Our melee_1 stood alone at (74,63): adjacent to their melee and within one of two ranged, taking about 420 a tick, with our nearest healer at (76,59) — range four, no heal at all — while **of our five ranged exactly one was within three of any enemy** (ranged_5 at (76,62)); their hurt melee went M8A5 -> M8A8 in two ticks and our fire netted zero (their total 16 094 -> 16 200 over t=73–94). The chain: the rows anchor on the *most forward* melee (v23); `engage` sends each melee at its own nearest enemy with standoff 1; melee_1 charged five cells in ten ticks, the ranged row (two behind the anchor) and the rear (one behind) jumped with it and lagged two to four cells; once hurt it rotates toward a healer — and stays put (`step=stay`), because the cell scoring pays `PAIR_W_OUTGOING` = 30 for a cell adjacent to an enemy against `PAIR_W_DIST` = 10 per step toward the target. **Five eliminations were tried on the bot and every one was rejected by the stub (206 scenarios; baseline 9 failures, 125 annihilations, no army lost).** *A withdrawing creep (rotating, wounded, alone in fire, leashed) scored as non-aggressive*: 61 worse, 40 better, failures 9 -> 12, annihilations 125 -> 111, m25 sleeper **our army lost** at 1020. *Withdrawing = rotating or wounded only*: 50 worse, 44 better, failures 9 -> 10, annihilations 125 -> 123 — a wash. *Only the adjacency reward removed for a withdrawing creep*: 48 worse, 25 better, failures 9 -> 11, **two armies lost** (m17 wing at 658, m24 army at 803). *A heal leash — a melee in combat never steps beyond `HEAL_RANGE` of every healer*: 72 worse, 36 better, failures 9 -> 13. *A melee out of the line rejoins the mass before anything else*: 75 worse, failures 9 -> 14, m23 army lost at 631, total margin −160 000. The stub is consistent about it: a melee that leaves contact costs more than a melee that dies in it. **And the stub cannot reproduce the loss.** The new `screen` script is the live block as closely as the log allows — healers one cell behind the front and never stepping away, ranged in the front row firing from one to two cells, melee never advancing without a healer within two, no rotation, every gun on our most forward creep, the block standing once any of ours is within three — and in all three forms it is destroyed on every map tried (29, 28, 25, 22, 18, 12; t=246–513), usually without a loss of ours. It kills our front melee at the live rate (four ticks) but our fire nets about 250 a tick against it where live it netted nothing; that gap — what the live block does that `screen` does not — is the open item, and until it closes the stub cannot judge a fight-execution change. The map is `map-match29.txt`: eleven of twelve scenarios pass (`spread` in the grind), `screen` runs on maps 29, 28 and 25. Result: our army destroyed by t=200 at 3967:3896, points 1680:4444 at t=600, enemy losses one scout and, later, one melee.

**Match 30 (05.09.2026, v28's fourth series — lost at once, rating 1022 -> 1018; player 2 from (85,88), against the match-28 opponent):** the whole army destroyed by t=160 and the enemy lost **nothing** — zero deaths, two melee disarmed (M4 and M5 hulks by t=88 and t=97) and healed back to M8A8 by t=160, its five ranged and three healers never hit once. **The third live loss exactly at the parity floor (27, 29, 30), and all three share one script:** we take R3 at t≈40 (3967 against 4087 = 0.97), the enemy takes no flag at all — here it walked straight past D5 — and comes to us at R3 in full strength; HOLD from t=40 to t=68 with retreat feasible, contact at t=68, the fight accepted at 0.97 and lost twelve to nothing. **The positions at t=80 say what the fight actually was.** Their melee stood *three* cells from ours and never engaged; our melee held the line (`so=0`, target their own cell) and never struck — and had already lost ten attack parts between three of them (M8A5, M8A4, M8A5) to *ranged fire from three cells*, then their fire moved to our ranged and healers (M6R3/R4/R5 and M6H3/H4 by t=93). It was a standing ranged duel: 5 × 60 = 300 for them, 5 × 60 × 0.8 = **240** for us, equal heal, and neither side's melee in it — while `powerOf` counts our melee at full (`meleeFactor` is 1.0 whenever the enemy has melee), so the "0.97" that admitted the flag was 0.8 in the duel that was fought. Our fire went where the focus ranks it — their front melee, the highest threat by `threatOf / killTicks` — disarmed two, and both were healed back; their healers and ranged stood within two or three of several of our ranged at t=80 and ranked below the melee. **One elimination was tried and rejected by the stub:** *no first flag against an active enemy that holds none* — `CAPTURE_FLOOR` (1.0) instead of `PARITY_FLOOR` (0.97) unless the enemy is passive or owns a flag. The fights loved it — 84 scenarios better, m18 block destroyed at 188 instead of 721, a dozen points wins turned into annihilations — and the points races hated it: 71 worse, **failures 9 -> 14**, every one of the fourteen a `spread` or `farm` race and seven of them gate scenarios, total margin −223 000. The dispersed opponent takes its flags late, the gate waits for its first, and we lose the start of the race. What stands: three live losses at 0.97 against an active unflagged army, all as a ranged duel at 240:300, and a capture estimate that counts melee at full for a fight in which neither side's melee engage. The map is `map-match30.txt`: twelve of thirteen scenarios pass, `farm` reproduces the points-loss family again (18183:23626) and goes to the grind. Result: our army destroyed by t=160 at 3967:4087, points 324:183 at t=200, enemy losses none.

**After match 30 (05.09.2026) — the operator's question, "find a way to beat these opponents; perhaps split the army into groups and run them as separate groups":** answered as a plan, `docs/pain-and-gain-groups.md`, and by two pieces of measured work. *The instrument first.* The `screen` opponent was rebuilt eight times against the positions of matches 29 and 30 until it moved as the live block does: two rows fixed on its melee centroid, ranged at the ends of the front row and the healers directly behind, marching as one block that advances only while formed, holding exactly three cells from our nearest armed creep, giving ground to one that steps to two, healers adjacent to whoever is being hit, every gun on our most forward creep, and its melee entering only on a creep its ranged have already disarmed. Each earlier form lost to v28 on every map, and the decisive gaps were found one at a time in the stub's own numbers: a column that formed under fire (its healers eight to twelve cells behind the first creep hit), ranged crowding the anchor two cells from our melee (which `holdMelee` strikes), and healers at range because the front row took the cells around the hurt creep (net damage 167 a tick = 240 of our volley minus 72 of ranged heal, where the live block healed 216). **The two-row screen beats v28 on map 30 — four of ours alive against eleven, 11639:21697 — and on map 25 it reproduces the standing duel of match 30 to the tick** (t=790–807: our hits 15 730 -> 14 902, theirs 14 400 unchanged). It still does not reproduce the ending: in the stub the duel is won by v28 twelve to nothing, because the block walks into our line on our first disarmed creep and loses the brawl there — live the same brawl was lost twelve to nothing; what the live block does in it that the script does not is invisible in our log. On maps 29 and 22 its formation gate deadlocks. *Then the charge.* All melee on the focus target in one tick once the screen has stood `STALL_TICKS` at three, with the melee within `FORM_RANGE` of their centre and a healer within `HEAL_RANGE`, and the ranged row one cell closer to reach the second row: on the whole suite of 222 scenarios the result is byte-identical — nothing but the map-25 screen holds a screen for twenty ticks — and there the charge fired twice, collapsed the block (enemy power 3247 -> 609 in thirty ticks) and finished it at t=1029 instead of v28's 1047 at the cost of **four creeps** (ten alive against fourteen). In the stub patience beats the charge; live, patience lost three of three. **Rejected in this form and recorded**: the charge cannot be judged until the instrument reproduces the live brawl, which is the plan's first step. The plan itself: groups as objects (STRIKE, FIRE, GARRISON) with the army logic per group, one coordinator that reads the opponent (screen, dispersed, farmer, army) and lays the groups out, the charge and the pincer as group actions with readiness and a single entry tick, and the garrison against the farmer. Result: no bot change; `screen` reworked and kept (map 30 in the grind as an open loss).

**Match 31 (05.09.2026, v28's fifth series — a win over Hardy in 1000 ticks, then lost to the match-28/30 opponent, rating 1021 -> 1017; player 1 from (12,9)):** the fourth loss at the parity floor and the same script to the tick — R3 (13,49) taken at t=39, HOLD until the enemy, holding no flag, arrived at t=68 at 3967 against 4087, the army destroyed by t=150 with **no enemy loss at all** (one melee disarmed at t=84 and healed back to M8A8 by t=134). This time the positions were read tick by tick, and two earlier readings of the family are corrected by them. *It is not a standing duel with a decoy*: their melee were adjacent to ours from t=76 on and moved almost every tick, working our ranged (ranged_3 at (13,40) disarmed by their melee from (12,41)); their hurt melee at t=80 had **no healer within three** — it needed none. *It is not a formation failure either*: every one of our melee had two or more armed allies within two at t=80. What our melee actually did: melee_3 at (15,45) stood (`so=0`) with the nearest enemy melee four cells away, because an enemy **ranged** at (18,42) stood within three — `holdMelee` fires on any combat enemy within three and then lets the melee strike only what is within two, so a ranged at three pins our melee in place while their melee work our line elsewhere; melee_1, under fire without two armed allies adjacent (`aloneInFire`), walked back to the armed centroid two cells from the enemy, where it neither struck nor left the fire. Their melee dance in and out at one to two cells and are never pinned. **Four eliminations were tried and rejected by the stub (222 scenarios; baseline 11 failures, 136 annihilations, no army lost).** *A melee is a live threat only when adjacent*: 37 worse, 19 better, failures 11 -> 13, annihilations 136 -> 123, m9 hunter our army lost at 973. *A melee at two that has stood still two ticks is not live*: 18 worse, 13 better, failures 11 -> 10 — and beside the point, their melee never stood. *The hold-the-line strike without the formation requirement*: 23 worse, 17 better, failures 11 -> 10, annihilations 136 -> 131 — beside the point too. *Hold the line only against enemy melee within three*: the first form that does not lose the sum — 37 worse, 37 better, failures 11 -> 9 (m12 farm and m28 farm from loss to win), total margin +161 000, no army lost — at the cost of six annihilations (136 -> 130, all still points wins) and one gate scenario, m20 farm, flipped from a win at 1924 to a loss at 1893. It is rejected on that gate line and recorded as the most promising form so far: it unpins the melee exactly where the live loss pinned them, and the stub cannot say what happens next because no stub opponent pins them (the two-row `screen` deadlocks or brawls). The map is `map-match31.txt`, and its scenarios join the suite. Result: our army destroyed by t=150 at 3967:4087, points 336:0 at t=150, enemy losses none.

**v29 — the doctrine "the first flag is theirs" (05.09.2026, on the operator's order after four armies lost in a row: "without a win we go no further; analyse every loss to this player and find a way to win, even by changing the strategy radically"):** the four losses (27, 29, 30, 31) share one script — we take R3 at t≈40, an army holding no flag arrives in a block at 0.97 and destroys us with no loss of its own — and the losses to the same player before that (28) show the other half: with seven flags it was at 2954 against our 4179 and we cut it from fourteen to six by t=210. **What is fatal is not the flag but standing on it.** v29 treats a massed, active, unflagged army closing on us (`enemyMassed`: two thirds of its armed within `MASS_RANGE` of its centre; `approachRate` at least `APPROACH_RUSH` = 0.5 of its speed toward us) as a superior one whenever it is not weaker than us (`EVADE_EQUAL_RATIO` = 1.0): the army evades instead of holding, and the flag is left to them — a chase at equal speed scores nothing, and every flag they take instead costs them 3–5% of their power until the fight is ours (the parity doctrine is not withdrawn but completed: parity is held against an enemy that pays for its own flags). The last call opens the flags at an *equal* score too, so a 0:0 is never simply given away. *The first form also refused the first flag while such an army existed and was rejected by the stub*: failures 12 -> 16, six gate lines — the dispersed and farming opponents also "close on us" for their first forty ticks and waiting for their first flag lost the start of the race. **The stub's verdict on v29 itself is mixed and cannot be decisive**: 235 scenarios, failures 12 -> 13, annihilations 144 -> 137, one army lost (m28 army at 681 — the brawl at 1.0 that flips on a tick either way), total margin +158 000; four gate lines red (m18 spread, m19 farm, m31 farm, m28 army). The stub's opponents are ones v28 already beats, and every stub-optimal choice lost to this player four times. **The closest model of him is now in the stub and is the evidence this version rests on**: `screen+flagless` — the two-row block with its runners idle, as the live opponent took no flag until our army was dead. Against it v29 loses nothing on any of the six live-loss maps (30, 31, 29, 25, 28, 22): the army evades, both armies stand at 14/14, and the match is won on points 5253:0 by the one flag we took before he came, an unreachable lead at t≈1790. Against the flag-taking `screen` v29 destroys the block on four of those maps and wins the other two on points. This version is committed on the branch for a live series and **not landed**: the gate measures the opponents we beat, and the decision it cannot make is the operator's.

**Match 32 and the first live series of v29 (05.09.2026):** the series opened with **a win over the match-28/30/31 opponent** — he went for the flags this time, held all seven by t=83 at 2954 against our 3967, and from t=57 the army pushed and killed thirteen of fourteen by t=820 (behind on points 7286:13297 the whole way; the win is the annihilation at t≈900); then **a win over the match-29 opponent** in 200 ticks; then a loss to the match-27 opponent, player 1 from (12,9), rating 1029 -> 1018. The loss is the rush script again — no flag until t=387, straight at us in a block — and this time the doctrine fired and **failed in execution, twice over.** The rush flag flickered: EVADE at 57, HOLD at 69 with `approach=84` (the column on the march stretched wider than `MASS_RANGE` and `enemyMassed` dropped), EVADE at 94, HOLD at 109 with `approach=42`, EVADE 117, HOLD 122, EVADE 126 — and contact at 127, a fight at 0.97, the army gone by t=230 (this one was closer than the four before it: all four of their melee and one ranged died; their four ranged and three healers finished our hulks). And the evade point was **home** — (12,9), the corner behind us — because `exitMargin` projects the pursuer along the *measured* approach rate, 0.42 in a pause of the column, and from there home looked safe; the army crept ten cells in forty ticks and was caught against the map edge at (5,41). **v29 as committed now carries both fixes**: the rush flag has hysteresis (entered at `APPROACH_RUSH` and `enemyMassed`, kept until the enemy takes a flag, turns passive, disperses, or is beyond `EVADE_RANGE` and no longer closing), and during a rush the pursuer is projected at full speed. On the stub the flagless chasers — `screen`, `nine` and `block` with idle runners on maps 30, 31 and 32 — are all won on points with the army intact (the map-32 `nine+flagless`, the shape of this very loss, 22850:1111 with thirteen alive against five), and those nine lines are now gate lines: the live opponent is in the gate. **The gate's verdict on the whole is unchanged and the operator's decision stands**: 257 scenarios, failures of the old 235 rising 12 -> 16, six gate lines red (m12 and m18 spread, m18, m19 and m31 farm, m28 army), one army lost (m28 army, the brawl at 1.0), annihilations up, total margin +211 000. The dispersed and farming opponents pay for the doctrine's caution in the first forty ticks; the rushing ones no longer kill us. The map is `map-match32.txt`, twelve of thirteen scenarios pass (`spread` in the grind). Result: series 2 wins then a loss with our army destroyed by t=230 at 3967:4087, enemy losses five.

**Match 33 and the second live series of v29 (05.09.2026):** the series opened with **a win over the match-27/32 rush** — the same player, the same script, and this time the evasion held: the rush flag stayed up, the army evaded south along the edge toward H4 instead of into the home corner, the block caught us at t=123 on its own terms and **lost the brawl at 0.97 twelve to nothing** (fourteen to eleven at t=130, four to one at t=170, two scouts by t=210; then all seven flags unopposed and an unreachable lead at t=1100). The same fight the same player won 12:0 in match 32 — two outcomes of one brawl, one each way, which is what the stub has said all along: at 0.97 the brawl is a coin, and the doctrine's worth is to toss it on our terms or not at all. Then a loss to the match-28/30/31 player, rating 1023 -> 1017 — and it is match 28 to the tick: he took all seven flags by t=82 (2954 against our 4179), we cut him from fourteen to four by t=960 at 4133 against 807, and then chased four kiters (one M8A8, three M6R6) for 540 ticks without a hit while they and two scouts took the flags back behind us — 11764:23313. The rush family is closed by the doctrine; the chase family stays open, and its lever is not another stop-the-chase rule (ten rejected) but the garrison. **The garrison was built and measured, and is rejected in this form.** With `PUSH_RATIO` dominance over the whole enemy and the rest of the army still dominant without him, an armed creep standing on our flag stays as its keeper with no enemy nearby, released when the flag is lost, he is at half hits, dominance goes, or a picket above `KEEP_PICKET` comes. The stub's `farm` never triggers it (the farming blob keeps its army, we are never dominant) — so the remnant was modelled, `+weak`: the enemy fields two scouts, one melee, three ranged and two healers, the shape the live opponent was left with. Against `farm+weak` the garrison turns two knife-edge wins into comfortable ones (m28 20324:19834 -> 23725:6087, m32 23006:21253 -> 23783:7282) — but **v29 without it already wins all five**, so the stub does not hold the live chase either, and on the whole suite the garrison is a wash that costs the gate: 23 worse, 31 better, failures 17 -> 18, two new gate reds — m32 farm from a win to a loss, and m20 kite from an annihilation to a loss where the army stood 1300 ticks at (21,50) while two enemy scouts out-scored it (a latent sweep defect the garrison's timing exposed: the march stall wants the centroid frozen to the cell, and a creep stepping back and forth defeats it). Recorded, not kept. **v29's live record so far: wins over all three players that beat v28 (4), losses 2 — one execution defect since fixed, one chase.** The map is `map-match33.txt`; its scenarios and `farm+weak` on maps 33, 28 and 32 join the suite. Result: series 1 win then a loss on points 11764:23313 with twelve alive against four, enemy losses ten.

**v29 landed (05.09.2026, the operator's decision — "do as you propose"):** the doctrine "the first flag is theirs" goes into `main` with its live record of four wins and two losses against the three players that beat v28 four times in a row, and the stub gate is redrawn around it. Six lines v28 passed and v29 loses move to the grind as the doctrine's known cost — `spread` on maps 12 and 18, `farm` on maps 18, 19 and 31 (the dispersed and farming opponents pay for its caution in the first forty ticks) and `army` on map 28 (the brawl at 1.0 that flips on a tick either way) — and the gate keeps the live opponent instead: `screen`, `nine` and `block` with idle runners and `farm+weak`, twelve lines v28 lost four armies to. The landing gate: 218 scenarios, none failing. Both families continue on live series from here: the rush family is closed by the doctrine, the chase family is open with the garrison measured and put down.

**Match 34 and the third live series of v29 (05.09.2026, the landed version):** a win over the match-27/32 rush again — the third in a row over that player since the evasion fixes — then a loss to the match-29 player, rating 1020 -> 1008; player 2 from (85,88). His rush was recognised at t=3 (`rush=true`, EVADE) and the evasion walked **toward him**: the escape candidates are the flags and the two homes, the exit margin rated R3 (85,49) safe because we would reach it first, and R3 lies on the axis of his approach — the army marched thirty cells north from (77,80) to (81,53) while he came down the same axis, our scout took R3 at t=43, contact at t=57 at (76,61), and the fight at 0.97 was lost over 450 ticks of mutual healing (their melee re-armed faster than ours; the army gone by t=550, their losses two). **The candidate set was the defect**: with flags between the homes there was no "away". v29 now adds the four map corners to the escape candidates and, during a rush, skips any candidate not farther from the enemy's armed centre than we already are. On the stub the flagless chasers keep losing to it — `screen`, `nine` and `block` with idle runners on map 34 (the evasion fires one to three times, the army intact, 5253:0 / 23220:370 / 22621:580) and `screen+flagless` on the six older maps unchanged. The map is `map-match34.txt`; its scenarios and the three flagless chasers on it join the suite. Result: series 1 win then our army destroyed by t=550 at 3967:4087, enemy losses two.

**The healing finding (05.09.2026, the operator's hypothesis from the match-34 log: "our healers mostly rangedHeal, theirs heal adjacent"):** measured from the per-tick hits of both armies — the engine's hits per tick are heal minus damage, so a rising delta is healing net of damage and its size names the kind (72/144/216 adjacent at 12 a part, 24/48 ranged at 4). Over the match-34 brawl (t=57–550) both sides took the same damage, **42 285 against 41 684, and we healed 29 275 against their 39 420** — 35 % less with the same three healers; "+144" events (two healers adjacent to one creep) 22 against 46, healing events 407 against 570; purely ranged deltas (24/48) about equal on both sides. Control: the won brawl of match 33 was 7 719 against 1 310 (their healers were cut off), match 31 was equal healing and three times the damage taken (focus, not healing). **Where our healers stood**: sampled every ten ticks of that brawl, adjacent to the most wounded ally in 13 % of samples, two cells away in 33 %, three in 22 % and beyond healing range in 32 %; of the ticks when the most wounded stood inside enemy reach, two thirds had no healer adjacent to him. The mechanism is not the row slot (a healer with a wounded mate already targets him — a patch putting that branch before the slot changed nothing on 289 scenarios) but the **cell bans**: a support creep may not enter enemy ranged reach and may not stand adjacent to any enemy, and in a brawl the wounded stands exactly there. v29 now lets a healer with a wounded mate within healing range accept ranged fire and stand next to him; only cells adjacent to an enemy *melee* stay closed. On the stub the brawl outcomes churn as always (23 worse, 23 better), the aggregate improves — failures 18 -> 15 against the evade-away build, no army lost more — and where the rule fires the healing is what it should be (m30 nine: healed 3 012 -> 5 022, "+144" events 3 -> 12); the stub's own blocks barely heal (512 and 176 a brawl), so the live effect is for the live series to show. The same batch carries the evasion fixes of match 34 — an escape point must be farther from the enemy's armed centre than we are and the corners join the candidates during a rush (keeping the previous point while no candidate is ready, meant for the corner-hopping of m28 hunter, changed nothing on 289 scenarios and is not kept) — and the outside research of `docs/pain-and-gain-research.md`, whose first item is the same target-selection question and whose fifth is the tool that would close the stub's blind spot: full replays with the enemy's attacks and heals.

**Match 35 (05.09.2026, the first match of the healer batch, lost to a new opponent, Coldkimchi, rating 1008 -> 996; player 1 from (12,9)):** the third distinct failure of the evasion's execution, and the first where the doctrine's bet did not pay at all. He took **no flag for 450 ticks** — no D5, no R3 — and did not rush either: he hovered by D5 and A3 with the whole block, closed when we stood and paused when we moved, a patient herder. The rush flag came and went with his pauses (`approach=0` for a hundred ticks at a time), and every re-entry picked a new corner — (12,9), (3,3), (96,3), (8,90), (3,96), (90,8) — so the army zigzagged across the whole map and at t=450 was caught **in its own home pocket** at (5,9): walls on two sides (x ≤ 4 for y ≥ 8, the rows above narrow to six cells), his twelve in a line across the mouth at y=16–17, ours inside at y=9–14. Of our five ranged two to four had a target within three, **none of our four melee was adjacent to an enemy at any sample** — his melee stood at exactly three, `holdMelee` strikes only what comes within two, and the line stood under fire; the fight at 0.97 (the scout had taken R3 at t=44) was lost in fifty ticks with two of his creeps scratched. Their damage taken 1 703 against ours 9 536; their bodies never lost a part. **The healer fix did what it was built to do**: our healing over the fight came as 72-events (36) far more than 24-events (19) — adjacent healing — but there was nothing to heal against 220 a tick with 40 coming back. The lesson is not one more evasion rule: on a bounded map at equal speed a patient block herds an evader into a corner, and the two places this bot loses are the same two the research names — a line that stands at three under fire without striking, and fire that does not land. The standing-line charge measured and put down earlier (all melee on the focus target after `STALL_TICKS` of a screen at three) was built for exactly this tick and never fired on the stub; it has now fired live, once, against us. The map is `map-match35.txt`; its scenarios and the two flagless chasers on it join the gate (all twelve pass). Result: our army destroyed by t=500 at 3967:4087, enemy losses none.

**v30 — the press (05.09.2026, built on replays of both sides).** Our console logs show our intents and the enemy's positions, never the enemy's shots, swings or heals; `arukuka/screeps-arena-tools` fetches a match's full replay through the client (SIGUSR1 opens its Node inspector, the replay chunks are downloaded with the client's own session), and `tools/replay.py` reads it. Six replays (matches 30–35) answered the question the stub could not. **Fire discipline and focus are not the problem**: every ranged creep of ours that had a target within three and did not fire was already disarmed (bodies are `r6m6`/`a8m8`/`h6m6` with the weapon parts IN FRONT, so 600 damage strips all six RANGED and 800 all eight ATTACK); the enemy fires exactly when it has a target (50→51 %, 64→64 %, 61→62 % of ranged creep-ticks), we fire in 43–61 % with targets in 53–74 %; focus share 0.64–0.83 against their 0.71–0.87, and the winners even spend 30–50 % of their shots on disarmed hulks. **The losses are geometry.** The enemy's line stands at exactly three from our front (their ranged→nearest distance histogram in match 35: 2:49 3:234 4:141; ours 1:18 2:75 3:111 4:80 5:35 — our row across a diagonal axis puts half the ranged at five and half at two), and its melee do not stand at the line: they walk around our front to our ranged and healers — match 30: 66 adjacent creep-ticks and 55 swings against our 28 and 18 (their a→ranged 17, a→healer 15; ours a→ranged 1), match 31: 54/46 against 18/12, match 35: our melee adjacent 0 ticks of 244 on start-of-tick positions. A melee of ours three cells from a line under five ranged (300 a tick) did nothing: `holdMelee` strikes what comes within two and their melee never do, `aloneInFire` pulls back whoever steps first, so the pack never forms (match 30, t=90: of three healthy melee one held the line, one 'withdrew to the mass', one charged alone). **The press** (`USE_PRESS`, `PRESS_RANGE`=3, `PRESS_PACK`=2, `PRESS_PATIENCE`=3): when the enemy line has stood at range for three ticks — contact, their fire reaches us, no enemy melee within three of any armed creep of ours — every melee with a melee mate within two (or already adjacent) goes adjacent to the focus target, or to the nearest catchable armed enemy within three; the ranged keep their row; once on, the press holds while contact lasts. Two forms were rejected on the stub with numbers: **the ring** (each ranged at exactly three from the focus, no row: the enemy's melee each found a nearest target of their own, the damage spread over six of ours instead of two and six creeps were disarmed by t=80 — m18 rush lost the army against a clean win at t=100, 74 lines worse, m13 sleeper army lost) and **the press from the first tick of contact** (against a charging enemy three melee stepped out to meet it and were disarmed together in five ticks, m18 rush t=66–71; a charger walks into our focus by itself — the press is for the one who stops at three and shoots). The patient press fired in 173 of 304 stub lines and cost nothing the fights could measure: the two gate reds it exposed were latent defects of v29 — a lone disarmed creep with an enemy within eleven threw `NoSuchElementException` (`localAllies.maxOf` on an empty list) every tick to the end of the match (m19 nine stood 1500 ticks after a won fight; v29 threw the same in m8 rush t=115–120 and m28 wing t=1200 and the stand reported `errors: 0` — it now counts every crash trace), and the formation vanguard was chosen against ANY combat enemy, so a surviving healer trailing the army at seven cells made the rear creep the vanguard and the formation pulled the army back one step for every step toward the flag (m13 rush, 140 ticks at (20,21)); both fixed structurally (`maxOfOrNull`, the formation forms against `threatening` enemies). Stub result: 286 of 304 lines pass (v29: 288), no crash in any run and no army lost; the one gate red, m29 farm, is the farm family's coin flip (byte-identical to v29 until t=970, one cell of centroid at t=980, the press first fires at t=1570) and joins m20 farm in the grind, so the family is watched by the three farm+weak lines and the grind; 52 lines worse and 51 better, nearly all of them the remnant chase after a won fight flipping between 'destroyed' and 'lead' in either direction; m28 hunter, an army LOST in v29's grind, is a win at t=233.

**Matches 36–38 (05.09.2026, the first live series of v30, landed and tagged `pain-and-gain-v30`): 2:1 — a win over けろびー (rating 986 -> 998), a win over stachu3478 (998 -> 1002), a loss to けろびー (1002 -> 996).** The press fired in every fight (match 36 at t=168 and 193, match 37 at t=74, 95 and 107, match 38 at t=61 and 79). Match 36: the fight at 1.02 went from twelve against twelve to nine against three combat creeps by t=350, the enemy took no flag at all and the match ended on an unreachable lead at t=1200. Match 37: the healer-heavy opponent of match 34 was destroyed by t=200 with all twelve of ours alive at 16 000/16 000. **Match 38 is the first loss with both sides' intents in hand** (`tools/replay.py` on the fetched replay): the melee phase (t=60–80) was even — his four melee charged into our press and both sides' melee were disarmed together (his melee adjacent 81 % of creep-ticks but 83 of 106 as hulks; ours 68 adjacent ticks and 36 swings against 9–18 in the losses before the press) — and the ranged duel that followed (t=80–110) was lost on **target selection**: he fired at our RANGED (70 of his 131 shots; 98 over the fight) while we fired at his HEALERS (49 of our 77; 77 over the fight), who stood adjacent to each other and healed it all back (147 adjacent heals × 72 = 10 584 against our whole fight's 10 440 of ranged damage). A disarmed ranged is minus 60 a tick for the rest of the fight; a healed healer is nothing. Our ranged spent 84 creep-ticks disarmed, his 8; 288 shots against 174; and we fought with R×0.8 the whole time — our scout took R3 at t=42, twenty ticks before contact, through the parity floor by 3967 against 3964 (the power model counts melee at full strength although they are disarmed in the first twenty ticks; the same scout grab happened in all three matches). His other numbers, for the record: 58 mass attacks against our 14, focus share 0.78 against 0.70, 93 shots at disarmed creeps against 36. **v31 — the healer's threat is its live output.** `HEALER_VALUE` was 3.0 ("a killed healer is the one loss the enemy cannot heal back", match 9), which made a healer 216 of threat against a ranged creep's 60 and sent the focus into the healer cluster; the replay puts a healer's live output at the same 49 a tick as a ranged creep's (0.68 × 72 against 0.81 × 60), and the mutual healing the multiplier was meant to weigh is already in `killTicks` (`healOn`). Now a healer counts its heal parts in full only with a wounded mate adjacent (12 a part), a third otherwise (4 a part at range), and the multiplier is 1.0: a ranged creep at the front (60 / 4.8 ticks) outranks a healer in the cluster (72 / 7.7 ticks), which is what the winner does. The scout's R3 before the fight is the next open finding: the parity model's price of −20 % ranged fire is 3 % of power, and the fight is decided by ranged fire. Stub: 287 of 304 lines pass (v30: 286), no crash, no army lost and no gate red; against v30, 52 lines worse and 36 better — the worse ones are slower sweeps of the stub's kiting remnants (kite/roost/hunter lines 30–200 ticks slower, its kiting healers are no longer the focus), the better ones faster kills of sleeper, army and fourteen.

**Matches 39–43 (05.09.2026, the first live series of v31, landed and tagged `pain-and-gain-v31`): 4:1 — 欢欢 (996 -> 996), stachu3478 (996 -> 1000, destroyed by t=100), 欢欢 (1000 -> 1001), stachu3478 (1001 -> 1004), then a loss to けろびー (1004 -> 1000) in 200 ticks.** The loss is a different けろびー — the client names it v2 against the v3 of match 38 — and a different shape: a standing line. His four melee stood two to three cells from ours for forty ticks and did not strike (adjacent in 7 % of creep-ticks, 8 swings in the whole fight); his five ranged, at three behind them, fired at OUR MELEE (132 of his 180 shots), one at a time as each came within reach: after the evasion our melee were the tail of the column and came forward one by one — the first went 1480 -> 710 in five ticks (t=61–66), then the next (1600 -> 830, t=67–70), then the third (1600 -> 630, t=72–76) — while the other three stood four to six cells back. The forward melee was `aloneInFire`, and its answer, 'back to the centroid at standoff two', was already satisfied two cells ahead of the mass, so it stood there under five ranged. The press stayed off until t=104 because its trigger read 'an enemy melee within three of our armed' as an attack, and a melee that stands at two or three is a screen, not an attack. Damage 5:1 against us in the first ten ticks (16 000 -> 14 568 against 16 000 -> 15 702), the army gone by t=200. **v32**, two computed rules in place of that one: (1) an enemy melee is fighting only when ADJACENT to one of ours, and their melee are coming only when their distance to our armed shrank by `PRESS_CLOSING`=2 or more over the patience window (a charger crosses three cells in three ticks, a creeping screen one) — without the second half the adjacency trigger sent the press out to meet the stub's charge (m34 rush: press at t=62, the exchange 16 000 -> 9 440 against 14 307 without it); (2) a press target that keeps its distance for two ticks of the chase is dropped for `PRESS_GIVEUP`=20 ticks — the stub's screen, which steps away from our armed at two, turned the press into a chase under its ranged and cost the army (m35 screen). Two answers to the trickle itself were measured and rejected: **the wall** — melee without a target taking slots in the anchor's row so the four come forward as a line — froze melee in the block whenever no enemy was within three, so remnants were no longer hunted (rush/block/nine lines from 'destroyed' to 'lead' by the dozen, m3 sleeper's army lost, m4 kite and m33 farm+weak red; 282 lines against 288 without it); and **the regroup** — a lone melee under fire going adjacent to its nearest melee mate instead of 'to the centroid' — sent the melee that was finishing a fleeing remnant alone back to its mate, and the remnant got away (m34 farm 21744 -> 9237, 48 lines worse, 286 against 288). The lone melee's stand two cells ahead of the mass stays an open finding: the press pack is the pack, and the pack forms from the block, not from the retreat's column. Stub: 288 of 304 lines pass (v31: 287), no crash, no army lost and no gate red; 16 lines worse and 18 better against v31, the sleeper lines faster by twenty to fifty ticks.

**Match 44 (05.09.2026, the first match of v32, landed and tagged `pain-and-gain-v32`; lost to けろびー, rating 1000 -> 997; the client names him v1 — a third shape of the same player).** The fight was WON at t=180: after a hundred ticks of evasion the block met his rush at 1.0, three of his four melee were dead by t=180 and we stood at 16 000/16 000 against his 12 800/12 800 — thirteen against twelve, power 3970 against 2812. Then the parity model said 'push' (1.3 and more) and the army did what pushing is: the block dissolved, every creep hunted the nearest catchable enemy on its own, and his line did what a ranged line does against a chase — retreated eighteen cells in eighty ticks firing from three (his ranged at exactly three in 226 of 455 samples; ours at three in 155 and at four in 141): 311 of his shots against 175 of ours (68 % of his ranged creep-ticks with a shot against 44 % of ours), 137 of them at the melee that led the chase. The exchange turned within sixty ticks and the army was gone by t=280 at 10 against 9. Our fire was also spread — 1.91 distinct targets a tick, 66 of 192 shots at healers although `HEALER_VALUE` is 1.0 now: a ranged creep whose focus target is out of its range shot 'the most wounded in range', and healers are what is wounded and in range behind a retreating line. **v33**: (1) pushing pays or stops — the push needs the exchange over the last `STALL_TICKS` to be at least even (`PUSH_EXCHANGE`=1.0: hits taken off the enemy against hits taken off us, both sides' healing inside); Lanchester grants the right to push, the exchange says whether it pays, and when it does not the block re-forms; (2) a creep whose focus target is out of its range takes the FIRST target of the army's focus ranking that is in its range, ranged and melee alike, instead of the most wounded. Stub: 289 of 304 lines pass (v32: 288, the best count so far), no crash, no army lost, no gate red — every remaining red is a spread or farm line in the grind; 77 lines better and 59 worse against v32, both sides mostly the remnant sweep flipping between 'destroyed' and 'lead' and kills that got faster or slower by tens of ticks.

**Match 45 (05.09.2026, the second match of v33 — played from the branch commit, not landed, as rule 5 allows; the first was a win over けろびー in 100 ticks, 997 -> 1006; then a loss to けろびー v3, 1006 -> 1000, in 300 ticks).** The melee brawl was even again (both sides' melee disarmed by t=95, our 28 swings to his 28); the ranged duel was lost the same way as match 38 and for the same reason with a new face: **he fires at our ranged, we fire at his healers.** His shots in t=70–125: r→ranged 96, r→melee 64, r→healer 12; ours: r→melee 69, r→healer 46, r→ranged 24. Our ranged spent 37 creep-ticks disarmed, his 15; his ranged stood at 1 189–1 194 hits at t=100 while ours were `M6h`/`M4h`. `HEALER_VALUE`=1.0 had not moved the share: a healer 'live' beside a wounded mate weighed 72 against a ranged creep's 60, and across the four losses to him our healer share was 24 %, 34 %, 39 %, 26 % of shots against his 0–18 %. And for the fourth time in four fights with him our scout took R3 at t=43, twenty ticks before contact, while the army was already evading his rush (`approach=100` from t=35): −20 % ranged fire for the whole fight, through the parity floor by three points of power (3967 against 3964; the power model counts melee at full strength although they are disarmed in the first twenty ticks). **v34**: (1) no flag for anyone while `unflaggedRushNow` — the signal that sends the army into evasion now also closes the scouts' captures: the fight is twenty ticks away and the debuff would be paid in it; three points a tick for thirty ticks against a fifth of the fire; (2) a healer counts a third of its heal in the threat, always (24 against a ranged creep's 60): it is shot when it can be killed this tick (the focus's first tier) or when no armed enemy is in the creep's range (`focusOrder`), which is the winner's share. Stub: 286 of 304 lines pass (v33: 289), no crash, no army lost and no gate red; the cost is on the stub's kiting families — with its healers shot last the kite sweeps got slower (m33 kite 368 -> 778, m12 kite 219 -> 408) and three farm lines of the grind flipped — while the live evidence of four fights says the healer-first fire is what loses the ranged duel.

**Match 47 (05.09.2026, the first match of v34, from the branch; lost to けろびー v1, rating 1000 -> 997, in 200 ticks — the fifth loss to this player in a row while every other opponent is beaten).** Two things the replay puts beyond doubt. First, the scout took R3 at t=42 AGAIN, past v34's rule: his army took D5 at t=39 on its way in, so the rush was no longer 'unflagged' (`noEnemyFlag` false), `unflaggedRushNow` dropped, and the capture gate opened three ticks before the scout reached R3. Second, the shape of the loss is the trickle once more, in numbers: our melee e_1 alone two cells from his line at t=62 (its mates at three, three and six), 1300 -> 1120 -> 940 -> 400 -> dead by t=66 under five ranged focusing it (his r→melee 62 of 99 shots); then e_2 and e_3 went in and reached adjacency at t=69–70 with e_4 still five cells back; his line stepped back one cell a tick as they came, so our ranged, two behind our melee, stood four to five from his (58 % with a target against his 66 %). Damage 5:1 against us in the first twenty ticks (16 000 -> 11 714 against 16 000 -> 15 217) with R×0.8 on us and D×1.1 on him; without the R3 grab the ranged duel arithmetic flips (5 × 60 × 1.1 = 330 a tick against his 300, instead of 5 × 48 × 1.1 = 264). **v35**: (1) captures close on ANY imminent massed fight, flagged enemy or not — `fightImminentNow` = the rush signal without its 'no enemy flag' clause (evasion keeps that clause: a flagged enemy is weaker and is fought); (2) the regroup with a computed condition — a lone melee (no melee mate within two) goes adjacent to its nearest melee mate when the fire on its cell over `REGROUP_TICKS`=5 ticks is at least its hits, i.e. it cannot survive five ticks where it stands; a remnant's fire never reaches that, a line of five ranged does (the unconditional regroup was rejected in v32 for pulling the melee that was finishing a remnant back to its mate). Stub: 285 of 304 lines pass (v34: 286), no crash, no army lost and no gate red; the stub again pays for the regroup on its remnant sweeps ('destroyed' to 'lead' on block/rush/sleeper lines — the match is still won on points) because its ranged do not focus a lone melee the way けろびー's do; the live series is the arbiter.

**Matches 48–51 (05.09.2026, the first live series of v35, from the branch): 3:1 — けろびー beaten in 200 ticks (997 -> 1009), stachu3478 twice (1009 -> 1013 -> 1016), then a loss to けろびー v5 on points, 15 652 : 22 950 at t=1600 (1016 -> 1015).** Match 48 is the first fight against him won outright, and won the way the arithmetic said: no flag taken before the fight (he took D5 at t=42, `fightImminentNow` kept our scout off R3), the block met his rush at 1.0 with the ranged at full 60, and by t=120 he had six combat creeps left against our twelve at 15 305/16 000 — by t=140 16 000/16 000 against his seven. Match 51 is a different game: his v5 is a farmer. He took six flags by t=80 (himself down to 0.75 power), hovered ten to twenty-eight cells from us and never fought — the replay has no shot of ours in 1 600 ticks and ten of his at a healer — while we stood in ANNIHILATE facing him on two or three flags against his five, 8 points a tick against 17. Neither stall net saw it: the picket needs an enemy within `ENGAGE_RANGE`, the march net needs a centroid that does not move, and the army kept walking after him. **v36 measured a third stall net** — in ANNIHILATE without contact, an enemy beyond `ENGAGE_RANGE` whose distance to our armed centroid has not shrunk over `CHASE_WINDOW` ticks keeps its distance, a stall like the other two — **and switched it off**: it sends the army to the flags, but the race is not won by leaving the chase — the wandering blob walks back onto what we leave. On the stub the farm family moved both ways (+6 354 m12 farm, +12 871 m23 spread, −6 986 m25 farm, −7 742 m33 farm+weak), the kite sweeps got 60–100 ticks slower, and m32 farm+weak — a live-opponent line — went from an unreachable lead at t=1557 to a loss on points; 283 lines against 285. The farmer stays the open finding it has been since match 26: not 'stop chasing' but 'keep what is taken' (the garrison was rejected at 17 -> 18) or intercept the blob. `v36` plays as `v35` with the net off (`USE_KEEPS_DISTANCE_STALL`). Stub: with the net off the build is v35's — 285 of 304 lines, no gate red; with the net on, 283 and one live-opponent line red.

**Matches 52–54 (05.09.2026, the first live series of v36 — landed as the batch v33–v36, tagged `pain-and-gain-v36`): 2:1 — けろびー beaten twice (1015 -> 1026 -> 1037), then a loss to Coldkimchi, the patient herder of match 35 (1037 -> 1028), in 700 ticks.** His line does what match 35's did, now with both sides' intents in hand: it stands at three, steps back a cell a tick from every press (ten presses between t=88 and t=262, ten give-ups), never sends its melee in (adjacent in 4 % of creep-ticks, 7 swings), and its ranged fire at our front — r→melee 68, r→ranged 61 — 135 shots against our 95, a target within three in 62 % of its ranged creep-ticks against 47 % of ours. No flag on either side until our army was gone (his first at t=267). The cause is the order of the rows: our block is led by the front MELEE (the anchor), the ranged row stands `3 − d` behind it — right for a charger, whose melee walk into our focus, wrong for a line that kites: the melee never reach it, only drag the formation forward and become its targets, while our ranged, one or two behind them, stand four to five from his. Both けろびー and Coldkimchi stand the other way round — ranged and melee in one row at three, healers eight behind. **v37 measured the other order** — when their melee are not coming, the row led by the RANGED at `RANGED_RANGE` from the nearest threat, melee and rear one cell behind — **and switched it off**: against the stub's kiter (five ranged and two healers, the melee bait gone) the ranged in front are the first disarmed — 600 damage strips all six RANGED, a melee holds 800 and lives 1 600, so against pure fire the melee ARE the better front: m13 kite lost four of our five ranged by t=280 with the melee untouched and the army by t=500 (v35: the enemy destroyed at t=409). The two discriminators tried did not separate Coldkimchi's standing line from a kiter with the same ranged at three: 'their melee are not coming' alone put the ranged in front of every fleeing remnant (71 lines worse, m28 farm+weak red), and 'their distance has not grown over `APPROACH_WINDOW`' holds for a kiter we keep following. The layout stays in the code under `USE_RANGED_FRONT`; the standing line that kites from the press is the open finding. What v37 keeps: the give-up on a target that keeps its distance (v32) now covers every melee engagement in the block, not only the press — the melee that stepped toward his ranged at two, which stepped back, is the same chase in miniature. Stub: 286 of 304 lines pass (v35: 285), no crash, no army lost and no gate red; 31 lines worse and 36 better against v35, the worse ones the stub's kite sweeps, which no longer chase a target that steps back.

**Matches 55–56 (05.09.2026, the first live series of v37, from the branch): 1:1 — G1N6ERbreadMan beaten (1028 -> 1030), then lost to (1030 -> 1019) in 300 ticks.** Two defects, both with numbers. **The scout took R3 at t=49 while the army had been evading his rush since t=3**: `captureAllowed` said no (the runner was `POISED`, keeping one cell from the flag), but the path to that cell was computed with the runner's own flag cell open (`crowdMatrixOf(ctx, flag)`), so the path ran THROUGH the flag and the step onto it captured it — the same mechanism as in the four けろびー fights before v34/v35's rules, which had closed the decision but not the path. **The press went in against melee on the approach**: at t=115 his four melee were four to seven cells from ours and closing; the trigger saw no melee adjacent and no distance shrinking by two over its window, and sent the pack in; three ticks later his melee arrived at our pack, standing at his own ranged — his 51 melee swings against our 24, 31 of them at our ranged, mass attacks on the packed block — and five of ours were gone in twenty ticks (16 000 -> 6 624 between t=113 and t=140). The press was built for a line whose melee do not fight (けろびー v2, Coldkimchi: melee at two or three, 4–7 % adjacent); a melee at four to eight is neither that nor absent, it is a charge in position, and against a charge the block that waits wins (match 33 against the same player). **v38**: (1) a poised runner's path treats its flag as a wall; (2) the standoff count does not run while any enemy melee is between `MELEE_HOLD_RANGE + 2` and `ENGAGE_RANGE` from our armed — their melee are either at the line, or far, or coming. Stub: 284 of 304 lines pass (v37: 286), no crash, no army lost and no gate red; the stub's chargers (nine, rush, sleeper) are finished later — against a charger without focus the press into the approach paid, against G1N6ERbreadMan's it cost five creeps, and the live match is the arbiter.

**Matches 57–58 (05.09.2026, the first live series of v38, from the branch): 1:1 — Hardy beaten (1019 -> 1022), then the farmer again, けろびー v5, on points 10 804 : 22 779 at t=1400 (1022 -> 1016).** Six flags by t=82, 1 400 ticks beside us without a shot, our twelve at full strength against his four at the end, and the race lost the same way as match 51. This time the reason is in our own gates: with our power at 3 900–4 100 against his 2 700–3 000 the parity floor left room for four or five more flags, but `captureAllowed` closes on 'contact' and, since v35, on 'a fight is imminent' — and a farmer who hovers four to ten cells away, approaching and backing off, is in contact and imminent all match long while never fighting. **v39 measured the relaxation** — an enemy with combat creeps within reach whose fire has not taken a hit off us for `STALL_TICKS` ticks in a row is not fighting, and the two fight gates do not apply to it — **and switched it off**: the stub's farmer does not fire either, the gates open, the army takes flags down to parity with HIS full army (debuffing itself to 0.97 of him) and spreads over flags he walks back onto — m33 and m28 farm+weak, live-opponent lines, from green to red, m29 farm 23 825:19 258 -> 19 386:23 822, m24 farm 20 033 -> 8 958. Neither the third stall net (v36) nor the open gates (v39) win the race against a farmer; what does is a chase he cannot leave — pinning him to a wall, hunting as a fan, splitting the army — the research's items, next. The stub's gate is also cut this day (see the harness section): 120 lines in 45 s instead of 304 in three and a half minutes. Stub: with the relaxation off the build plays as v38 — the gate's 120 lines green in 45 s; with it on, two live-opponent farm+weak lines red.

**v40 — the intercept (05.09.2026, the research's second item, first half).** The farmer cannot be caught at equal speed and does not need to fight, but he scores only by standing on a flag's cell, and he goes for the nearest flag that is not his. So when an enemy with combat creeps has been within reach of the army for `STALL_TICKS` ticks without taking a hit off us AND keeps beyond `ENGAGE_RANGE` (a standing screen within reach is a fight, not a farmer — the first form without that clause turned m31 screen, a fight v38 won at t=439, into an 800-tick standoff on our own flag), the army goes to the flag he must take next — the one nearest his armed centroid that is not his — and stays there: on our own flag as the post, on a neutral or his flag as the objective, with the capture gates open for that one flag only (v39 opened them for all and the army spread to its debuff). Pushing, which against him is the chase, is off while he is that. The stub cannot judge it — its farm blob steps away from any armed creep within six and never needs the flag cell, so the farm family moved both ways again (m33 farm+weak +10 229, m29 farm 23 825:19 258 -> 16 863:23 820, m30 farm +975); the gate is green. The second half of the item — cappers from the scouts and the disarmed hulks, and the pincer from two sides when he stands on the flag — waits for a stub model of the live farmer (six flags in proximity order by t=80, hovering ten to twenty-eight cells off, walking back onto what we leave), which is the research's fifth item. Stub: the gate's 120 lines green (m33 farm+weak +10 229 against v38); in the grind the farm family moved both ways (m29 farm from a lead to a loss, m30 farm better).

**Match 59 (05.09.2026, the first match of v40, from the branch; lost to the farmer けろびー v5 on points 9 359 : 24 080 at t=1400, 1016 -> 1010).** The intercept engaged and whipsawed: 'the flag nearest his armed centroid that is not his' changed with his every step, and the army walked between (8,90), (31,67), (49,49) and (13,49) — forty to sixty cells apart — arriving nowhere (FLAG/HOLD flips every ten to thirty ticks from t=222) while he farmed 17 a tick; the kills began only at t=900 (13 -> 4 of his by t=1300) with the gap already 6 168 : 14 771. **v40c**: the intercept flag is the first flag in his order (by distance to him) that WE reach first — our travel by the flow field plus `INTERCEPT_MARGIN`=2 ticks no more than his distance — and it is sticky: held until he takes it. The stub's gate is green (120); the rest is the live match.

**Matches 60–67 (05.09.2026, the first live series of v40c, from the branch): 7:1, the best series so far — けろびー (the farmer, match aborted on his side at t=400 with the score 1 617 : 6 952 against us; not our doing), stachu3478 twice, Hardy, G1N6ERbreadMan, 欢欢, and けろびー's fighting version beaten outright (no flag on either side until his army was gone at t=250, twelve of ours at 16 000/16 000), rating 1010 -> 1043; then a loss to けろびー v2, the standing line of match 43, in 300 ticks (1043 -> 1038).** The loss has the shape of matches 43 and 53 with the numbers of the replay: our melee disarmed his four melee by t=100 and were disarmed in return (the press went in six times and gave up six times as his line stepped back), but his five ranged stood at 1 200 the whole fight while ours were stripped one by one — 305 shots against our 189 (his ranged fired in 69 % of creep-ticks, at exactly three in 190 of 415 samples; ours 56 %, at three in 108 of 323 and at four in 83), his fire on our melee (149) and ranged (85), ours on his melee (85), healers (57 — a third of our fire, though a healer weighs a third since v34; the 'killable this tick' tier and the per-creep fallback still reach them) and ranged (35). Two open findings from this session meet here: the line that kites from the press (the ranged-front layout was rejected on the stub's kiter, v37), and the healer share of our fire. The intercept (v40c) was not exercised — the farmer's one match aborted.

**v41 — a melee is a full threat only when adjacent; the mixed ranged-and-melee row measured and left off.** The operator's reading of match 67 was a front: the enemy stretches his nine armed creeps into a line so every gun fires, ours stand in a clump that blocks itself. The replay gives him the width (9.7 cells across the axis to us against our 4.6; match 53: 5.7 against 4.2) — but not the consequence: our ranged had a target within three in 67 % of their armed creep-ticks against his 55 %, fired in 58 % against his 56 %, and of our ranged creep-ticks spent at four or more from any target 42 had a free cell ahead and 6 a friend — nothing was blocking them, they stood one row further back. Focus was equal too (1.74 targets per tick and a focus share of 0.73 against his 1.91 and 0.72). What differed was the volume — 535 shots against 258 — and it came from longevity: his ranged lived armed 875 creep-ticks against our 413, because we never shot them. Resolved on start-of-tick positions, of the 125 shots our ranged fired with one of his ARMED ranged within three, 49 went into it, 39 into a melee and 37 into a healer; of his 172 such shots, 112 went into our ranged. The same split in the other two losses to standing lines (match 53, Coldkimchi: 58 of 160 against his 147 of 211; match 43: 30 of 65 against 66 of 107) and, weaker, in the wins (55 % and 49 % against his 55 % and 77 %). The cause is in `threatOf`: a melee within two of any of ours counted as live at its full 240 — four times a ranged — so the comparator's 'threat per tick to kill' sent the line's fire into the melee standing at two, which his healers stood behind and rebuilt (his four melee were disarmed by t=100 and back in the line, armed, by t=150), while his ranged stood at 1 200 the whole fight. Over the fight those four melee swung 56 times — 0.06 a tick, 13 damage — and his five ranged fired 480 times, 34 a tick each. v41 counts a melee at its full threat only when it is adjacent to one of ours, or at two while it is CLOSING — its distance to our nearest combat creep smaller than a tick ago; a melee standing at two takes the kite discount like one at three (the focus is recomputed every tick, so a melee that steps in is the full 240 the tick it arrives). The plain discount at two, without the closing test, showed on the gate why the test is needed: the standing lines improved (wing +9 938, +2 846, +1 446 in margin) but five annihilations of chargers turned into leads on score (rush on maps 30, 32, 34, 35; nine and fourteen on 30; block on 29 and 35; army on 5) — a charging melee at two is adjacent next tick, and a tick of unfocused fire on it was a price the stub could see: 12 lines worse, 9 better. The formation idea was tried first — one row of ranged and melee (ranged in the middle, melee on the flanks, rear one behind), under `USE_RANGED_FRONT`: the stub's kiter stripped the ranged again (m13 kite: army lost, 2 of 9 alive at t=1094; m32 farm+weak red; 11 lines worse, 6 better), and the narrowing 'at least two of his melee within eleven' passed 120/120 with not one line different from v40c — it fires nowhere on the stub, and in match 67 it would have fired in 6 of 150 contact ticks, because his melee hover at two to three the whole fight. A condition that never holds is a label, not a fix; the row stays under the toggle, off. Gate: 120/120; against v40c 6 lines worse — five annihilations turned into leads (block on 29, rush/nine/fourteen on 30, rush on 34: the fight is won by t=100 either way, and what walks off is the remnant of three healers at speed parity, which huntable does not count — the same coin lands the other way on 29 rush, 31 nine and 34 nine) and wing on 34 down 1 942 in margin — and 8 better: wing on 28 up 9 938 and on 17 up 3 202, three leads turned into annihilations and three faster ones. Full regress 284 of 304 against v40's 281: six red lines turned green (grab on 1, farm on 29 and 30, spread on 29 and 35, screen on 31), three turned red (farm and spread on 18, sleeper on 31 — a farm race lost 17 703 : 18 350); 46 lines worse and 52 better, the chargers' annihilation-or-lead coin landing both ways about equally, and the stub's wing 4:4 — its melee hold at three, where the discount already applied, so the stub has no opponent that stands its melee at two the way けろびー v2 and Coldkimchi do; the replays are the evidence, the live series is the test.

**Matches 68–70 (05.09.2026, the first series of v41): 2:1 — o4kapuk in 300 ticks, けろびー's fighting version in 1 300 (rating 1038 -> 1049), then a loss to けろびー v5, the farmer, 3 174 : 22 934 in 1 100 ticks without one shot fired by either side (1049 -> 1048). v42 — the intercept stops the push only while there is something to intercept.** He took six flags by t=100 and sat on D5 with all twelve (at 0.6 of his power for it: 2 954 against our 4 179), his five other flags unguarded, his rate 22 a tick against our 3 from the one flag our scout held. Our army stood ten to thirteen cells from his block for a thousand ticks. The log shows why: `pushing` was on (1.41 is above the 1.1 asked for when behind) but flickered with `enemyNotFightingNow`, whose 'enemy within reach' half toggles at the eight-cell boundary as his creeps shuffle on the flag — the posture switched ANNIHILATE / HOLD every one to four ticks (t=554–646: fourteen switches), and every HOLD tick sent the army to the `post`, which the intercept had set to our own R3 thirty-five cells behind: with all six of his flags his, the 'first flag in his order that is not his' was ours. Five creeps ended up at that post, the armed centroid moved back with them, the leash held the front, and the cohesion hold (286 of 1 320 creep-ticks) and standing steps (507) did the rest. No stall net fired in 1 100 ticks: the picket wants at most four catchable in reach and he had twelve flickering between catchable and not; the march stall wants the centroid in one cell for fifty ticks and it was jerking; the keeps-distance net is off. The v40c rule 'a farmer is not chased, he is intercepted' assumed a flag left to deny him; when he has them all he is not going anywhere, and against a parked enemy at 0.6 of his power a 1.41 army that stands is choosing to lose 3 to 22. v42: the intercept vetoes the push only while the intercept flag is not ours (a flag to deny) or we are ahead on score; behind, with nothing to deny, the army pushes — and if the parked farmer kites away from it, the picket net turns the push into captures of his unguarded flags as before. The intercept flag is now chosen before the push decision. The flicker had a second source, `huntable` dropping to 0 of 12 whenever his blob stepped two cells back and returned: `evasive` called a creep evasive once it had moved more than a cell away from where our centroid was eight ticks earlier, so one two-cell step made all twelve uncatchable for eight ticks (t=196–207, 223, 234, 554), the push released and the army turned to the post. That source stays open: three ways of closing it were measured and every one of them cost the stub's kiters and farm races — HOLD with the enemy near holding the line where it stands instead of walking to the post (58 lines worse, 48 better, the gate's m33 farm+weak and m29 farm red: the walk back to the post is what finishes a repelled rush in dozens of lines); evasive redefined as 'more than half the window moving away' (66 worse, 44 better, every kiter caught later because it is chased longer, five spread and two farm lines from lead to loss); and the push releasing on 'nobody catchable' only after a whole chase window rather than one tick (51 and 51, the gate's m28 farm+weak and m30 camp red, kiters later on ten maps, m16 kite lost). The quick release on 'nobody catchable' is what keeps the army from chasing a kiter; its price is those ticks against a blob that steps back and returns. In match 70 the first source accounts for most of the HOLD ticks (t=163, 234, 263 and the whole t=300–650 run were vetoes with twelve catchable), so the live series is the test. The stub got a new script for him, `camp`: the farmer until nothing is left to take, then the whole blob parks on the centre flag and never moves. On the five maps where it actually parks (29, 30, 31, 33, 34) v41 and v42 both destroy it between t=392 and t=703 with the same ticks to the digit — the stub does not reproduce the live freeze, whose ingredient was the far post pulling the army back every HOLD tick after the formation had stopped it eleven cells short; on 28, 32 and 35 our army leaves flags unoccupied and the blob keeps walking back onto them, which is the farm race again (identical scores). The five parking lines join the gate. Gate 125 of 125 (the five parking camp lines included), the same as v41d but for two small differences on map 31; the full regress 284 of 309 with the same red set as v41d plus nothing.

**Matches 71–72 (05.09.2026, the first series of v42): 1:1 — G1N6ERbreadMan in 1 100 ticks (1048 -> 1051), then a loss to けろびー v5 again, 12 135 : 23 807 in 1 500 ticks (1051 -> 1045) — this time with his army destroyed.** He farmed six flags by t=100 (his rate 21 against our 0); v42 pushed as intended, and the push worked: his fourteen were eleven by t=300, eight by t=700, three by t=900 and two by t=1 400, ours twelve at 16 000 the whole match. The score went the other way because flags are owned until someone else stands on them, and his two or three survivors kept four to six of them while our whole army of eleven walked to one corner flag at a time (FLAG objectives D5, A3, R3, D5 in fifty ticks at t=907–962 as the posture flickered HOLD / ANNIHILATE / FLAG on `huntable` 0 or 1 of 3) or hunted the last two (t=1 400: ANNIHILATE with 4 117 against 317, his rate 20 against our 5). The keepers of v2x stay on a flag only while an enemy is within ten of it and at most two armed near — a rule written for an enemy army, not for its remnant; the parity doctrine, likewise, keeps the army together against an army that can annihilate it. With his whole remnant weaker than one of our creeps neither applies: a creep left on every flag we take cannot be beaten by anything he has left, and the army that keeps walking is the five that remain. That is v44; the stub's `farm` and `spread` lines (red on eleven and six maps) and `farm+weak` are its measure.

**v43 — the standing fight is placed by cell, not by row; v44 (keepers under overrun) measured and withdrawn.** The operator's next idea: in a fight, first compute the ideal placement of every creep from healer reach, incoming damage and the ability to attack, then assign the creeps to those places — a planned fight instead of creeps deciding one by one; pursuit stays per creep. `planFight` scores every passable cell within a shot of any of ours — incoming damage (`damageAt`), his armed within three, the focus target within three, his melee adjacent and within two, distance to the nearest threat — and each role picks by the order of those facts, not by weights: a ranged wants a target within three, then no melee of his adjacent, then none within two, then the focus target, then distance three over two over one (without a target: closer), then less damage, then less walking; a melee without an enemy adjacent screens a ranged from the cell in front of it; a healer stands adjacent to the fighter taking the most damage on his cell, itself out of his melee's reach; the disarmed stand beyond a shot, next to a healer. Greedy assignment, the most constrained first; the previous tick's cell is kept while it still holds its role's top facts. Four gates measured the way there. Unscoped and without memory: 122 of 125, m28 kite army lost — reach 0/5 for a hundred ticks: with no cell offering a target the distance tier tied at three and 'less walking' kept the ranged where they stood while the kiter backed off a cell a tick and shot the melee following it. With 'closer when without a target': 120 of 125, m30 wing army lost — three healthy melee sent to the post 25 cells away by a HOLD tick while the wounded and the ranged stayed in contact (the post problem of match 70 on another coin). With memory: 122 of 125, no army lost, 29 lines worse and 19 better — wing ×4, block+flagless ×2 and farm+weak on 33 (+7 561) better, but ten block/nine/rush annihilations turned into leads and seven kiters were caught later: the free placement is looser than rows behind the front melee, and a charger's remnant walks off it. Scoped to the standing fight only (the press's standoff: his line under our fire, his melee not coming; a charge and a chase keep the rows): 125 of 125, 5 worse (kites on 4, 20 and 30 slower, wing on 29 down 7 503, block on 35 a lead) and 9 better (block+flagless on 32 lead to annihilation, farm+weak on 28 and 32 up, kites on 8, 29 and 33 faster). That is what plays. v44 was the answer to match 72 — under overrun (his whole remnant weaker than one of our armed creeps by Lanchester) every creep of ours on our flag keeps it and the capture gates open, so the army disperses over the flags by itself — and it was withdrawn before a match: the stub's farmer never lets itself be destroyed, so no line changed; and the model gives a lone creep zero power against any remnant with a healer (√(dps − heal) × hits), so the rule would not have fired in match 72 either. The real shape is groups sized by his remnant and by the army's reach to answer — the 'split the squad' item of the research, still open. The per-ten-tick line now prints `reach=a/b`, armed ranged with a combat enemy within three over all armed ranged.

**Match 73 (05.09.2026, the first series of v43): 0:1 — annihilated by Coldkimchi's standing line by t=240 with his army at 16 000 of 16 000 (1045 -> 1038).** Not a shot of ours stuck: our 255 shots were 15 300 damage against his 22 272 of healing (259 adjacent heals, 151 at range), his 362 shots 21 720 against our 19 320 — with three healers a side, single-target fire at 60 a shot barely dents a healed target, and the fight was decided by the melee: his swung 46 times (11 000 damage) and ours 7, his melee adjacent to one of ours in 40 creep-ticks, ours in 14 — his melee stepped up to our ranged and healers, swung and stepped back, ours held the line at two and never reached. v41's targeting held (with an armed ranged of his in reach we shot it 48 % of the time, up from 39 %, he 77 %), and 39 of those shots still went into an adjacent melee at its full 240 — right on its face, and hopeless at 1 600 hits behind 216 of healing. The placement of v43 knows the answer (a ranged wants no melee of his adjacent, then none within two — out of their reach and inside its own) and never ran: it was scoped to the press's standoff, and the press wants his melee NOT adjacent, so the one fight that needed it was the one that switched it off. The log also showed the planner assigning our own creeps' cells to each other (t=120: ranged_1 sent to ranged_3's cell and back), a carousel under fire: cells under one of ours are now that one's alone (gate 125 of 125, 6 worse and 10 better against v42). Scoped wider — the standoff, or contact while his melee are not closing on us — the placement answers Coldkimchi's dance and leaves a charge to the rows. Wider scoping was measured and rejected (123 of 125, wing on 28 and camp on 31 lost, 21 worse and 16 better against the standoff-only placement: once a charge stops, the placement takes the fight and the remnant walks off), so the dance is answered per creep instead — an armed melee of his adjacent to one of our ranged, healers or wounded is the target of our nearest melee within reach, over the line's hold at two: gate 125 of 125, 8 worse and 11 better, the annihilation coin both ways. That is v43b.

**Matches 74–78 (05.09.2026, the first series of v43b): 4:1 — Xeon_Lagunas, けろびー's fighting version (his fourteen were seven by t=300 and one by t=700, ours 13 600 of 16 000), stachu3478, G1N6ERbreadMan, rating 1038 -> 1057; then Coldkimchi's standing line for the third time (1057 -> 1050), annihilated by t=440 with his army at 16 000 of 16 000.** The replay is match 73 again to the number: his melee swung 48 times, ours 7 (adjacent to an enemy in 40 creep-ticks against our 17); with an armed ranged of his in reach we shot it 34 % of the time and he ours 77 % (140 of our 245 shots went into a melee standing on our ranged, 69 into healers); his focus share 0.79 against our 0.74; 228 adjacent heals against our 237.
