The poker rule of v43b did not produce swings: he swings and steps back, and our melee arrives a tick late — the kite in melee form. And one thing the log adds: the fight started at t=320 with the score 1 276 : 0 — we held three flags, were at A×0.8 R×0.8 D×1.1 for them, evaded his approach from t=160 (EVADE / HOLD) and were caught at equal speed; from contact there is no retreat, and a 'parity' fight at 0.8 against a line with three healers is 16 000 : 0. Three matches (53, 73, 78) say the same thing: the Lanchester model calls this fight even and it is not — single-target fire at 60 a shot into 216 of healing is cancelled unless four guns land on one target, and the decisive damage is melee swings, which his line delivers by stepping up to our soft creeps and stepping back, and ours never delivers holding a line at two. The doctrine question this opens is not a placement: whether to take flags at all while a full healer line is within reach of contact (the parity floor of 0.97 lets a flag through, and the 0.8 it costs decides the fight), and whether contact with such a line at parity should be evaded rather than accepted — the retreat that 'from contact there is no retreat' forbids at equal speed. Open. Also measured and withdrawn this evening: v44b, detachments — while the rest of the army keeps the push margin over his remnant, the weakest armed creeps join the runners one per unmanned flag and take flags by the scouts' logic. Full regress 270 of 309, 121 lines worse, nine gate lines red (roost, camp, wing, nine, farm+weak; spread on 28 at 108 : 24 322): detached creeps walk off into the map while the army is still fighting or forming, and the runners' flee logic scatters them further. The remnant race stays open with the shape named — groups sized by his remnant and by the army's reach to answer, not individuals.

**v45 — focus fire: a sticky focus target; the row built around it and the target his healers are far from, measured and left off.** The operator's reading of match 78: 'no focus fire — each ranged shoots its own opponent; keep them together and knock the most out of one enemy per tick, not a bit out of many', and then: 'choose the target his healers are far from, so they cannot heal it adjacent and lose a tick moving'. The replays measure it: the largest number of our shots landing on ONE target per tick was 1 in 57 of 111 ticks with shots, 2 in 42, 3 in 10 and four or more in 2 (1 %) in match 78 — 1 % in match 73 and 2 % in match 67 too — against Coldkimchi's 11 % and けろびー's 14 %. The focus-share statistic (0.74 against 0.79) had hidden it: with two shots a tick the share is high and the damage is nothing. With 216 of healing a tick on a target only four or five guns landing together strip anything, and we did that in one tick of a hundred. Three causes, three changes, each measured alone on the gate against v43b. The focus target changed from tick to tick (no hysteresis: hits and range to the centroid broke the ties), so every switch restarted the healing race — now it sticks while it is alive with a weapon or heal and within a step of some ranged of ours, and yields only to a target killable this tick: 125 of 125, 16 lines worse and 25 better — wing on 29, 32 and 35 and block on 34 and 35 from a lead to annihilation, block+flagless on 34 up 4 910, seven kiters caught sooner; this plays. The ranged row stood across the axis to the enemy group's centroid, so each of the five reached whoever stood in front of it and the per-creep fallback shot that — a row built around the focus target (centred on its projection, on the row whose centre stands at three from it) measured 20 worse and 17 better, and a net-damage tier for the case when nobody is killable (the target with the least healing on it and the most of our guns in reach — the operator's 'healers far from it') 13 worse and 8 better; all three together 124 of 125 with m33 farm+weak red, 25 and 23. The two stay under toggles, off: the row walks the ranged away from the melee and a charger's remnant escapes. In the placement of v43 the focus target within three is now the first fact of a ranged. The per-ten-tick line prints `conc=sum/ticks` — the sum over ticks with shots of the largest number of our shots on one target — so the stub measures concentration directly. The stub also learned `+fast` for the screen (no formation gate, as Coldkimchi's line walks): even fast, the stub's screen loses to the block (m30 destroyed at 199, m31 at 318) while the live line wins three of three — the model gap is the open item. Gate 125 of 125 for what plays.

**Matches 79–80 (05.09.2026, the first series of v45): 1:1 — けろびー in 1 200 ticks (1050 -> 1061), then Coldkimchi a fourth time (1061 -> 1055), caught in the home corner at t=510 after 190 ticks of evasion and annihilated by t=630 at his 16 000 of 16 000. v46 — evasion by direction when no point is safe; a post with room to evade measured and left off.** The sticky focus did not concentrate anything: the largest number of our shots on one target per tick was 1 in 63 of 83 ticks, 2 in 17, 3 in 3, four or more never (his 11 % again) — the target stuck, but our ranged did not reach it together, and each shot what it could (conc 1.6 in the log against 2.2 on the stub's gate). Concentration is a placement question, and the row built around the focus target stays the open item (the stub's verdict 20 worse, 17 better was on outcomes, not on this metric, which now exists). The decisive defect is elsewhere and the evade log names it: from t=315 not one escape point had a margin — (96,3) with 13, then (3,3) at −40 straight through him, our flag (90,8) at −2, R3 at −7, home at 0 with exit 0, the corner (96,96) at 0 with exit 0, (3,3) at −26 — and the army walked to the least bad of them, into the home corner, as in match 78 it had walked into the H4 corner. Two things follow, and the stub judged them apart. When no escape point scores above zero, the army no longer picks a point at all: it flees by direction — the cell EVADE_RANGE away in the one of eight directions where his armed centroid ends up farthest, among directions that keep EVADE_RANGE from the edges (an edge halves the directions, a corner quarters them) — re-evaluated every five ticks like the points; on the stub it never fires (125 of 125 with not one line different — the stub's hunter never pins us), in match 80 it would have fired in six decisions of seven. And the post: it was the flag centroid, our only flag H4 at (90,8) in a corner, and the army stood on it for 200 ticks while his line was far; clamping the post inside EVADE_RANGE of every edge was measured and rejected — the army walks off the corner flag and the enemy army takes it by walking over it (block+flagless on 32 from 21 647 : 775 to 9 118 : 8 300, screen+flagless on 32 from 21 094 : 680 to 5 184 : 0, camp on 29 and 34 and farm+weak on 32 lost; 122 of 125, 35 worse and 30 better). The corner post stays open: what it needs is a keeper against the walk-over, not a walk away from the flag. Gate 125 of 125, identical to v45.

**Matches 81–83 (05.09.2026, the first series of v46): 2:1 — stachu3478 twice (1055 -> 1060), then けろびー's fighting version, which v43b and v45 had beaten cleanly, annihilated us by t=120 (1060 -> 1052). v46b — the flee direction must lead away from him and may run along an edge.** The evade log convicts v46's own flee: at t=62 the army stood at (29,6) by the top edge with his charge at (30,30), no escape point had a margin (best (90,8) at −10), and the flee point was (52,32) — the direction whose endpoint lay farthest from his centroid among those keeping EVADE_RANGE from every edge; by the edge every direction along it was forbidden, only south-east, south and south-west remained, and south-east ran diagonally past his army: contact at t=69, our hits 12 444 at t=80, 3 700 at t=100, none at t=120, his back at 16 000. Under v45 the same moment walked east along the edge to (90,8) and fought him on better terms. The flee now keeps a margin no larger than the one we already have (at an edge the directions along it are open), and a direction must lead away — its dot product with the vector from his centroid to ours positive; among those, the farthest endpoint wins. The replay also gives the fight's number: his largest number of shots on one target per tick was four or more in 25 % of his firing ticks, ours never (1 in 17 ticks, 2 in 16) — the sticky focus of v45 does not put guns together, and the row built around the focus target measured no better on the stub's new metric either (mean 2.29 -> 2.24, higher on 29 lines, lower on 32, gate 124 of 125): concentration is the open item, and the next step is per-tick data of who reached the focus target and shot what.

**Fire concentration, measured on the stub with per-tick focus diagnostics (05.09.2026, after match 83).** A debug line in `healAndShoot` prints, for every tick with shots, the focus target, how many of our armed ranged have it within three and how many shot it. Against the stub's standing line (m33 wing) the focus target is within three of 0–2 of our five ranged in 108 of 117 firing ticks (distances like 6,5,7,3,5), and three or more of ours shoot it in 8 % of ticks; against the charger (m35 nine) three or more in 55 %. Two remedies measured with the same instrument: the row built around the focus target raises the line's share to 19 % and cuts the charger's to 22 %; choosing the focus by reach (the target within three of the most of our ranged, stickiness only while within one gun of the best) leaves the line at 8 % and cuts the charger to 46 %. Neither approaches the four of five the operator asked for, and the reason is geometry, not choice: with the row held at three from his line, any one creep of his is within three of only the one or two of ours nearest along the row (Chebyshev: a row cell at offset k reaches a target three ahead only for |k| ≤ 3, and his line sits at three to four with its creeps spread), while four or five guns reach only a creep that has come FORWARD of his line — which is exactly what Coldkimchi shoots (our poking melee) and what gives him 25 % of ticks with four or more on one target. The levers left are doctrinal: the ranged row at two instead of three (all five reach the target ahead, and his melee reach them), or the focus on his poking melee in the tick it steps in (within three of the whole row; 800 to strip behind 216 of healing). Open, with the instrument in place.

**Matches 84–87 (05.09.2026, the first series of v46b): 3:1 — けろびー twice (one aborted on his side at t=100), stachu3478, rating 1052 -> 1073, the day's high; then the farmer けろびー for the fourth time, 13 350 : 22 770 in 1 500 ticks (-> 1072).** Both armies at 16 000 for the whole match: he took six flags by t=100 (his rate 22 to our 3), the push of v42 went at him, his blob gave ground and came back onto the flags behind it, our rate climbed to 10–15 against his 13–18 and never crossed for long. This is the farm race the stub's eleven red farm lines describe, and the open item stays what it was after match 72: flags are kept by standing on them, and a single army cannot both hunt a blob that will not fight and stand on five flags — groups sized by his strength, and the keepers to hold what is taken. The flee by direction of v46b did not fire (four evade decisions, all with a margin) and nothing in the series was lost to a corner.

**v47 — the standing fight by the closing of the armies' centres (the operator's signal).** The placement of v43 ran only in the press's standoff, whose 'his melee not adjacent' clause switched it off in every fight with Coldkimchi, whose melee are adjacent to our ranged by design; 'his melee not closing' (v43f) took a charge that had merely stopped and lost the remnant. The signal now is the armies themselves: the distance between the centroids of the two ARMED armies, kept over the press's patience window while in contact; closing by PRESS_CLOSING or more within it is a charge, not closing while the enemy is not retreating is a standing fight — and in a standing fight the placement runs, in a charge and in a chase the rows do. Gate 125 of 125; against v46b 9 lines worse and 8 better — six annihilations turned into leads (blocks and wings on 29, 32, 33, 35, camp on 34), farm+weak on 32 and 33 up 4 728 and 7 127, wing on 28 from a lead to annihilation — against 21 and 16 with two losses for the melee-based signal. Concentration on the stub unchanged (2.23). The live test is a standing line; the stub has none that beats us.

**Matches 88–90 (05.09.2026, the first series of v47): 2:1 — けろびー and stachu3478 (rating 1072 -> 1081, the day's high), then the farmer けろびー again, 12 107 : 24 007 with both armies untouched. v48 — an enemy with fatigue takes no step, and 'slower than us' is judged where he stands.** The operator watched the farmer drive his own creeps into swamp they could not leave and our army refuse to go and kill them one by one. The replay confirms it with the map from the log: at t=1120–1170 four of his melee and a healer stood on the swamp patch at (41–44, 70–72) with fatigue 16–48, four to seven cells from our armed creeps, for fifty ticks; our army had them as its prey (every creep's target was (43,71)), the plain between was open, and it crept one or two cells in twenty ticks. Two model errors held it. The danger model gives every enemy a step of approach (`enemyOrigins`: its cell and the eight around it), so a melee standing at two counted as reaching us — but a creep with fatigue above zero does not move this tick, and a body with half its parts MOVE steps once in five ticks on swamp; the cells at two and three from his stuck melee, where our ranged could have shot them for free, scored as under his blades. And `catchable` compared plain-terrain periods, so a creep in the swamp was 'as fast as us' though it was five times slower where it stood. Now an enemy with fatigue has no origins but its own cell, and catchable uses the period on the enemy's own cell. Gate 125 of 125, no line worse, camp on 34 up 4 314.

**Match 91 (05.09.2026, the first series of v48): 0:1 — Coldkimchi a fifth time (1080 -> 1074), this time a fight of 430 ticks (t=80–510) with the placement of v47 running, ending the same way. v49 — the sticky focus lets go of a target it cannot kill; the ranged row at two measured and rejected.** The replay: our 748 shots went 395 into his melee, 236 into healers and 101 into ranged (35 % into a ranged when one was in reach, his 71 %); four or more of our guns on one target in 0 % of ticks against his 7 %; his melee swung 50 times, ours 15; adjacent heals 770 against 727 — both sides healing everything, and ours the side whose creeps were stripped one by one. The sticky focus of v45 turned against us here: his melee pokes adjacent (a full 240 of threat, the focus goes to it), steps back into his healers, and the focus stays on it while it lives within a step of one of our ranged — 395 shots into 1 600-hit melee behind 216 of healing. Two releases measured. 'Let go of any target with infinite kill-ticks while a killable one exists' — 125 of 125 but 19 worse and 12 better, screen+flagless on 32 down 16 145, block+flagless down 7 640, camp on 34 down 9 294, seven kiters slower: the focus jumped at every step of his healers and the healing race restarted each time; rejected. 'Let go of a melee whose threat collapsed' — not adjacent and not closing, the same test threatOf applies — 125 of 125, 13 worse and 7 better (the annihilation coin on chargers, kite on 34 not caught, wing margins down 1 350 and 2 344): mixed on a stub that has no opponent poking and stepping back behind healers, and taken live as the operator asked — 'implement and try'. The row at two from the nearest threat, the other lever named after match 83, was measured in the plan worktree with the concentration metric and rejected: gate 124 of 125 (screen on 32 lost), 18 lines worse and 14 better, concentration lower on 38 lines and higher on 17 (mean 2.32 -> 2.19) — at two his melee reach the row and our ranged shoot from inside the brawl. Gate 125 of 125.

**Match 92 (05.09.2026, the first series of v49b): 0:1 — けろびー's fighting version, beaten by v43b, v45, v46b and v47 four times running, annihilated us by t=180 (1074 -> 1069); v49b withdrawn.** The fight was even to t=130 (we had stripped three of his, he one of ours), then our ranged went 3, 1, 0 in forty ticks. The one thing v49b changed was the focus release for a melee no longer adjacent or closing — and in a brawl his melee step in and out every tick, so the focus jumped on every step out, exactly what the sticky focus had been for. The stub had said the same (13 worse, 7 better); the live match said it in one fight. The release stays under a toggle, off; v49c is v48 with that toggle. The mechanism of match 91 — the poke behind healers holding the focus — stays open.

**Matches 93–101 (05.09.2026, the first series of v49c): 8:1, the best series so far — けろびー four times (the fighting version among them, in 400 ticks), stachu3478 three times, G1N6ERbreadMan; rating 1069 -> 1113 and second place on the arena; then the farmer けろびー for the sixth time, on points in 1 500 ticks (1113 -> 1105). v49c is v48 with the focus release off: the placement by the armies' centres (v47) and the stuck-enemy fixes (v48) played. The operator watched the replay of the loss: we drive his remnant into a corner, then turn around and walk to the centre without finishing it — v50 below.

**v50 — a picket is a minority of his armed creeps, never the bulk of his army.** The operator watched the replay of match 101: we drive his remnant into a corner, then turn around and walk to the centre without finishing it. The log names the rule: the picket net fired four times — 'picket of 1 / 3 / 3 / 4 armed in reach for 20 ticks without damage either way — flags until' +300 at t=161, 547, 895 and 1 256 — each time on a cornered remnant of three or four armed creeps with their healers, which the net, written for a few creeps screening an army elsewhere (match 20), could not tell from that army. Each firing sent the army to the flags for three hundred ticks and let the remnant walk out of the corner and back onto its flags; he lost nine creeps over the match and still won on points. Now the picket count runs only while the armed creeps in reach are fewer than half of all his armed: when half or more of what he has is here and catchable, this is his army, and the hunt goes on until it is finished. Gate 125 of 125; against v49c 6 lines worse in margin (wing on 32 and 33, block on 32, farm+weak on 33, block+flagless on 34, camp on 34, one to three and a half thousand each) and 5 better — four leads turned into annihilations (nine on 19, block on 28, wing on 31, block+flagless on 32) and farm+weak on 28 up 17 277: the remnant finished instead of walked away from.

**Matches 102–105 (05.09.2026, the first series of v50): 3:1 — stachu3478 twice, G1N6ERbreadMan (rating up to 1 112), then けろびー's fighting version, lost in the corner by t=300 (1112 -> 1106). 'No flag while a massed army not weaker than us after it is on the map' measured twice and rejected; the corner flag before a fight stays open.** At t=117 the army took H4 at (90,8) — healing ×0.75 for the rest of the match — with his full army fifty cells away and walking at us ((33,23) at t=100, (73,12) at t=150); contact at t=150 by the corner, 4 087 against 4 191 by the model after the debuff, the fight even to t=190 and lost by t=300. Matches 78 and 80 had the same shape (H4 in the corner, then evasion, then the corner); the same fights without a flag were won five times (66, 75, 79, 93, 96). The parity floor of 0.97 let each of those flags through because the model calls the fight even at 0.97 — and a debuff cannot be given back. Refusing a flag while a massed, active enemy army not weaker than us after that flag is on the map was measured: 123 of 125 with camp on 30 and 34 lost and the screen+flagless lines at a third of their points (34 worse, 36 better); narrowed to an army that is approaching, 123 of 125 again with camp on 30 and 31 lost and screen+flagless on 32 down 17 696 (30 worse, 32 better) — the stub's farmer moves massed and not yet weakened while he takes his first flags, and the rule starves us in exactly the race he wins. Rejected as written. What the three losses share and the stub cannot show is the corner: the flag taken was H4 at (90,8) each time, and the fight was fought there. Open, with the numbers.

**Matches 106–107 (05.09.2026, the second series of v50): 1:1 — stachu3478 in 100 ticks, then the farmer けろびー for the seventh time, 13 098 : 23 022 in 1 500 ticks with our army untouched. v52 — detachments against a farmer.** He never fired all match (his three losses were our fire in passing); he held four or five flags with the blob, we two or three; the army spent nineteen of its sixty-two posture changes in ANNIHILATE chasing a blob that steps away, and the picket and march nets fired twice. One army cannot both hunt a blob that will not fight and stand on five flags, and the split has been the open item since match 72. The signal that makes a split safe is the one the intercept already uses, held longer: an enemy within reach for PASSIVE_TICKS who has not taken a hit off us is a farmer — a fighter fires within a hundred ticks of contact. Under that signal, and not in contact, the weakest armed creeps join the runners one per flag none of ours stands on, as long as the core of the army stays not weaker than him by the parity floor; the runners' logic (the global matching by value, the flight from a hunter, the exit from a pocket) takes and holds the flags, and the detached ranged shoot what is in reach. The first shot he fires dissolves them. The first form of this (v44b: the set chosen by the push margin, no farmer signal) had sent creeps off during fights and formations and was rejected at 121 lines worse; this one leaves the gate green (125 of 125) and turns the stub's farm lines on 21, 25, 33 and 34 from red to green with every camp line green (full regress 291 of 309); the cost is the dispersed `spread` opponent — 19, 21, 32 and 35 red — where there is no blob and the detachments leave the army short against creeps that sit two to a flag. Live is the test.

**Match 108 (05.09.2026, the first series of v52): 0:1 — the farmer けろびー an eighth time, 19 875 : 23 751 in 1 800 ticks with our army untouched, and the detachments never fired: '0 detached' the whole match. The signal counted ticks with him within NEAR_RANGE and no hit off us, and the blob hovers at ten to twenty-eight cells, so the count reset before reaching a hundred. The match itself was a tie in rate from t=400 (our three flags at 12 a tick against his four at 13) lost on the first three hundred ticks (his 17–18 against our 7–8). v52b redefined the farmer as a hundred ticks without a hit off us since he was last within reach of a charge, with that reach no older than a hundred ticks — and the stub answered: three gate lines red (farm+weak on 32 at 4 502 : 18 848, camp on 29 and 30), because the camper does not fire either until we come to him, the signal fires during our approach, the core walks in three creeps short and does not finish him. So the detachments are off under a toggle with both measurements: the signal that would separate a farmer from a camper is the blob's own movement, and it is open.

**Matches 109–110 (05.09.2026, the first series of v52c): 1:1 — houshanyun on points in 1 700 ticks (rating 1105, unchanged), then けろびー's fighting version, annihilated by t=150 (1105 -> 1099), the third loss to his rush out of the home corner.** No flag on either side. His charge came at t=3 (approach 100); the evasion, from our start at (83,86), went to home (85,88), then to the corner (96,96) at −4, then had (3,96) at −46 and (8,90) at −40 — every point behind him — and the flee by direction found nothing: from a corner every direction that leads away from his centroid leaves the map. Contact at t=70 between (79,81) and (93,93), our ranged 4, 3, 1, 0 by t=140, his army at 14 400 of 16 000. The same rush met in the open was won five times (66, 75, 79, 93, 96) and lost three times in a corner (83, 92, 110). The doctrine of v29 — evade an unflagged army not weaker than us — was written for fights lost standing on our first flag; from a corner start it hands him the fight in the corner instead. Two answers measured. Fighting the unflagged rush at parity instead of evading (EVADE_EQUAL_RATIO raised to the retreat ratio): 121 of 125, screen on 34 and camp on 29, 30 and 31 lost, 30 worse and 29 better — the stub confirms v29 and it stays. v53: the flee by direction may go sideways when no direction leads away — a direction whose endpoint keeps at least EVADE_RANGE from his centroid, away-leading directions first — so that from a corner the army runs along the edge and the contact, if it comes, comes in the open; on the stub it never fires (125 of 125, not one line different), live is the test.

**Matches 111–114 (05.09.2026, the first series of v53): 3:1 — stachu3478, werwie, けろびー's fighting version (1 200 ticks, 1102 -> 1111), then Coldkimchi a sixth time (1111 -> 1104), annihilated by t=550. v54 — a point he cannot reach is unknown, not safe.** The shape of matches 78 and 80 once more: H4 at (90,8) taken at t=180, the army on the post at (89,7) for two hundred ticks, his line coming up from (77,82); and at t=333 the evade log shows the defect in numbers — `to=(96,3) score=536870903 arrive=536870903 exit=536870905`: his path to the corner came back as Int.MAX_VALUE / 4 (a flow field past the BFS budget), the arrival margin and the exit margin were both 'infinite', the corner scored as perfectly safe, and the army walked into it with him seventy-five cells to the south. The sideways flee of v53 never got its turn, because a positive best point exists before the flee is considered. Now a candidate whose pursuer-ticks are unknown is skipped, and an exit whose pursuer-ticks are unknown is no exit; with every point negative, the flee by direction runs along the edge. Gate 125 of 125, unchanged — the stub never overran the BFS budget on an escape point.

**Match 115 (05.09.2026, the first series of v54): 0:1 — stachu3478, beaten nineteen times today, won the standing fight along the top edge (1104 -> 1092). v55 — a melee charges only a target our ranged can reach.** The opening was the one of every win against him: his rush at t=3, the evade east along the top edge, contact at t=77 at parity (4087 against 4087). The replay shows where this fight parted from the won one five minutes earlier (match 113, 200 ticks): there his melee walked into our ranged's reach and died there; here his single ranged creep came to two–six cells of our melee and backed off one cell per tick, and three melee with no enemy within three (so not 'holding the line') took it as their engage target within eight and followed it five–six cells ahead of our ranged into his block of four melee and four ranged: one lost 1 200 hits in two ticks (t=86–87), the second was dead by t=93, the third by t=100 — three of four melee gone with the fight even at t=100 (12 140 against 11 160). The rest was arithmetic: two of his creeps disarmed by t=100 walked back to his adjacent healers (three at 72 a tick) and were re-armed by t=110 (his counted maximum back from 13 200 to 16 000), our shots concentrated four-plus on one target in 7 % of firing ticks against 16 % in the won match, and with one melee left his melee took ours apart to t=340. 'Under fire without two mates adjacent — back' fired only once the blows were landing. Now the eight-cell engage of a melee requires cover: at least MELEE_COVER of our ranged within RANGED_RANGE + 1 of the target, or the target adjacent to one of ours; a melee holding the line (enemy within three) keeps its two-cell rule unchanged. Gate 125/125 at 19 better / 24 worse — a fight rule always churns twenty to thirty lines each way (v52c 29/20, v53 29/30; a change outside the fight churns 0/0). Two narrowings were rejected by the stub: cover only for a target in a block (two other armed enemies within three of it) — 124/125, 13 better / 24 worse, m33 farm+weak red; cover only while not pushing — 123/125, 10 better / 19 worse, m28 farm+weak and m29 camp red. The 'destroyed -> lead' lines of the rush scenarios are not this rule: there the last creep (a healer at equal speed) escapes the chase in every variant, the fight being over at t=94. **Recorded, not fixed — the flee trembling:** at the top edge with him due south, 'away' was off the map and the sign of a tiny lateral offset of his centroid decided west or east every five ticks — (5,3) at t=56, (3,3) at t=61, (50,6) at t=71, (57,6) at t=76, the centroid (30,3) -> (28,3) -> (25,3) -> (27,3) -> (30,3) in twenty ticks. Ranking directions by distance with hysteresis passed the gate but churned it (25 lines worse, 29 better) and the same trembling is in all nineteen wins against him, so it is a finding, not the cause. Gate: 125/125 (v55).

**Matches 116–119 (05.09.2026, the first series of v55): 3:1 — Xeon_Lagunas in 1 200 ticks (1092 -> 1093), stachu3478 twice in 100 ticks (-> 1097), then けろびー's farmer a ninth time, 8 404 : 22 531 on points in 1 300 ticks (1097 -> 1089) at 16 000 of 16 000 on both sides. v56 — detachments switched on with the blob-movement signal.** The log has 'reach 0 of 5' at every hundred-tick sample and the posture flickering ANNIHILATE (pushing, 1–6 of 12 huntable) against HOLD (0 huntable) thirty times in the first five hundred ticks; the picket net fired twice (t=474 and t=1130, flags for three hundred ticks each) and the flag phase scored 3–11 a tick against his 14–22 — he held four to six flags at every sample, and our scouts' flags were retaken by his creeps walking over them. The replay shows what the army was chasing: his armed centroid moved 6–38 cells per fifty ticks, but it was the centroid of creeps sitting on different flags at once (t=37: a melee on R3, a ranged on D5; t=55: a scout on the far A3, a ranged on the near one), his largest armed cluster within eight cells being 4–7 of nine at most samples and at most half in 19 % of ticks. That is the case the v52 detachments were written for and their note names what they lacked: a farmer-not-camper signal, the blob's movement. v56 turns USE_DETACH on with the signal in three parts — no hit taken for PASSIVE_TICKS, his armed within reach since the last hit (the hundred-tick expiry that kept v52 from ever firing live is gone), and his armed centroid moved at least FARMER_MOVE (10) over FARMER_WINDOW (PASSIVE_TICKS / 2) ticks; a camper's centroid does not move, so the stub's camp lines that v52b lost stay out of it. The weakest armed creeps then join the runners one per unmanned flag while the core keeps parity, as in v52. The first build dissolved every detachment one tick after making it (m28 farm+weak: six detached at t=100, none at t=101 with 'hurt' set): the hurt detector summed hits over the army, which had just lost the detached creeps' hits, and read the drop as damage — it now sums over all our creeps. The log line `detach t=N` names each change of the detached set with the signal's parts. Gate 125 of 125 at 7 worse / 5 better: m33 farm+weak +9 245 and m13 kite from lead to annihilation on the better side; two camp lines slower, where the camper's blob passes the movement test while still walking to its parking place and one creep is detached until contact. **The stub's bait, recorded as a model gap:** a wing block standing seven cells off with one ranged poker walking to two cells of our nearest fighter and backing off when approached (stachu3478's shape in match 115) is destroyed by t=174–203 on maps 28–35 with 11–14 alive both with the cover rule and without it — the army walks into the standing line at parity and his front ranged are within our ranged's reach, so the poker never stands four to six cells ahead of the block as the live one did; the script is not in the gate. Gate: 125/125 (v56).

**Matches 120–126 (05.09.2026, the first series of v56): 6:1 — けろびー's fighting build twice in 200 and 100 ticks (1089 -> 1101), stachu3478 in 100, then Coldkimchi for the first time after six losses, 1 500 ticks (1103 -> 1112, rank 1), stachu3478 in 400, けろびー's fighting build in 1 100 (his army down to three healers by t=200, all seven flags ours from t=400, 1114 -> 1121), then けろびー's farmer a tenth time, 15 389 : 23 231 in 1 600 ticks (1121 -> 1119) at 16 000 of 16 000. v57 — the farmer is the one who never hit us, and the detachment persists.** The Coldkimchi fight had the shape of match 115 — the evade east along the top edge, the flee trembling west and east ((5,5) at t=54, (57,5) at t=64), contact at t=79 at 4 087 against 4 087 — and this time it was won: two of his creeps dead by t=110, four by t=170, ours 16 000 of 16 000 at t=180. The replay puts numbers on the difference: our melee were adjacent to an enemy in 1 % of their creep-ticks (31, thirty swings) and his in 0 % (7, six swings) — neither side's melee reached the other's line, so the fight was the ranged exchange, 467 shots to 372, and with an armed ranged of his within three our shooters put 85 % of their shots into it (52 of 61) against 39, 36 and 46 % in the three losses to standing lines that v41 was written from. The farmer match was the first live run of the detachments and they failed in a way the log names in 46 lines: two to four creeps detached, dissolved 1–45 ticks later with `moved=false`, over and over — his blob parks on a flag for two hundred ticks and leaves when we come (replay: his armed centroid moved less than ten cells per fifty ticks in 63 % of ticks, longest pause 209; in match 119, 26 %), so the movement signal is not farmer-against-camper. What the detached did while out: one POISED a cell from D5 with a lone creep of his sitting on it, three RESERVE with no flag at all (every flag near his blob filtered as 'the pack arrives first'), and the pool was the weakest creeps, ranged, which cannot kill even a lone healer (60 a shot against 72 healed). The race itself was close — 12 a tick against 13 for the middle thousand ticks — and lost in the first four hundred (3–7 against 18–22, six flags his by t=100 while we evaded). v57 keys the signal on the chase that does not close: the third stall net's measure (distance between the armies not shrinking over CHASE_WINDOW with him beyond reach), now also requiring his own centroid to have moved at least CHASE_WINDOW / 2 over the window (a parked camper we stop short of does not 'keep distance' — the stub's m31 camp detached two at t=498 without it), seen within PASSIVE_TICKS; a detachment then persists. Three signals were rejected by the stub before the fourth: 'no hit for a hundred ticks' (v57) let the kiter of m28/m31 — which bit at t=76–84 with its bait and came back for the split core — annihilate the army (4 970 : 23 907; 1 817 : 23 890), and melee-first detachments (v57) returned one by one under its fire (six dead in seventy ticks by D5), because the Lanchester parity that sizes the core counts the melee's damage in full against an enemy that never lets them close. The farmer is therefore the one who has NEVER hit us (lastHurtTick == 0) and has been within reach at least once; the first hit recalls the detachment for good. Two runner fixes ride along for whoever is detached: a flag with a lone enemy occupant weaker than the runner is a target, not a wall; 'the pack arrives first' is judged by power for an armed runner; and a melee runner strikes what is adjacent (healAndShoot only shot for runners). Gate 125 of 125 (v57), 6 worse / 7 better against v56. On the stub the signal fires only before the first exchange: six or seven detached at t=47–94 against the farm+weak remnant and recalled at its first hit 25–30 ticks later, one creep for 26 ticks against the camper still walking to its parking place — every stub farmer fights first, so the live farmer, who never fires, is the only test of the persistent detachment.

**Matches 127–133 (05.09.2026, the first series of v57): 6:1 — けろびー in 1 300 ticks (1119 -> 1126), G1N6ERbreadMan twice in 1 100, stachu3478 in 100 and 1 100, あぶらむし in 200 (-> 1134), then けろびー's farmer an eleventh time, 8 346 : 22 771 in 1 300 ticks (1134 -> 1132) at 16 000 of 16 000. v58 — 'he moves' is judged by his armed creeps; the camper's deadlock recorded.** Match 127 was the first live run of the persistent detachment: four to five creeps out at t=77–80 (never hit, in reach, the chase not closing), recalled at t=94 on his first hit, and the fight that followed broke his army (7 200 of 7 200 hits left by t=200). Its price showed next: his remnant of two to four creeps farmed six flags for four hundred ticks at 19–22 a tick against our 3–7 while the whole army chased it — a creep that has hit us once is never a farmer again — and the match was still won by annihilation. Match 133 was the camper of match 70, not the runner of 119 and 126: six flags by dispersed creeps by t=100, then all nine armed parked on D5 from t=200 to the end (replay: his armed centroid moved 0 cells per hundred ticks, largest group 9 of 9), 18 a tick against our 7. Our side of it, in order: the push of t=55–179 stopped forty cells short and the march-stall net turned it into 'flags until 479' with no flag the parity allows (obj=- for three hundred ticks); the push of t=479–566 reached twenty-one cells and flickered with huntable 0 of 12 while he stood still; at t=566 the detachment fired — `dry=0`, because the centroid of ALL his creeps moved with his two scouts running the map — and four ranged went out; the core of eight at 3 078 against his 3 084 could never push again (the push wants 1.1 when behind), the four sat POISED a cell from free A3 and R3 for seven hundred ticks because the parity floor forbids the capture at parity, and nothing on the board changed from t=600 to the end. Two doctrines cancel each other there: a detachment sized to parity leaves a core that cannot push, and a capture rule at parity leaves detachments that cannot capture. v58 fixes the signal — his movement is the centroid of his ARMED creeps — so a parked blob makes no detachment. Releasing a stall that yields no flag was measured and rejected: on the stub's m31 camp the stall released at t=536 gave seven hundred ticks of ANNIHILATE pushing at 3 679 against 1 209 without a kill (both centroids in one cell, reach 0 of 5) and 17 133 : 23 618, where v57's flag objective D5 at t=412–536 had walked the army into him and destroyed him at t=776. The open finding under both matches is older than the detachments: a push toward a parked blob does not close (match 70, 133; the stub's m31 camp at 3:1). Gate 125 of 125, identical to v57 on every line.

**Matches 134–139 (05.09.2026, the first series of v58): 5:1 — stachu3478 in 200, 100 and 100 ticks, 欢欢 in 1 100, けろびー's fighting build in 400 (1149 -> 1163), then けろびー's farmer a twelfth time and the worst of them, 3 174 : 22 931 in 1 100 ticks (1163 -> 1161). v59 — the capture parity counts our whole side, and the detach window is fifty ticks.** This time the signal was right in the letter and wrong in the timing: five ranged detached at t=207 with the chase 'not closing' over the eight-tick window — while over the hundred ticks before it the distance between the armies had shrunk from 40 to 12 cells; the core of seven at 2 918 against 2 954 could not push, he walked off to (40,65) and parked, and from t=300 to the end nothing on the board changed. What the five did for eight hundred ticks is in the runner lines at t=500: four POISED a single cell from free D5, A3, R3 and H4, the scout POISED at the other H4 — nineteen points a tick within one step and forbidden, because `captureAllowed` prices our side as `ctx.army`, the core without its detached creeps, and a core at parity may take nothing; before the detachment, at 1.41, the scouts had grabbers assigned. The detachment was eating its own capture budget. v59 counts every armed or healing creep of ours, detached included, on our side in powerAfter — the parity doctrine insures the side against annihilation, not the core — and widens the detach window from CHASE_WINDOW (8) to DETACH_WINDOW = PASSIVE_TICKS / 2 (50): the chase has not closed over fifty ticks and his armed centroid moved at least a quarter of the window. The eight-tick window with the side parity alone was measured and rejected (124 of 125): on m29 camp the one creep detached while the camper was still walking to its parking place took a flag, and the fight that had destroyed him at t=481 was lost on points 20 306 : 23 827. Behind this series' first loss and the last one stands the same open finding: a push toward a parked blob does not close (match 133 at 21 cells with huntable flickering to 0; the stub's m31 camp at 3:1 with the army standing between three groups of his at 4–8 cells for seven hundred ticks, the leash marked only four times — mechanism not yet named). Gate 125 of 125, 2 worse / 2 better against v58, no detach line in any gate log: the stub's farmers never keep the chase dry for fifty ticks.

**Match 140 (05.09.2026, the first series of v59): 0:1 — けろびー's fighting build (his v3), annihilated at t=160 in a fight at our home corner (1161 -> 1150). Recorded, not fixed.** The opening was the one of the two wins over the same build earlier today (matches 120 and 125): evade to (85,88) at t=3, the corner (96,96) at t=33 with score −4 (no flee direction exists from the home corner and the negative point is the fallback), HOLD at home from t=39, contact at t=70–71 at 4 087 against 3 896. We led the exchange to t=100 (10 888 against 8 242) and lost it in the next ten ticks: his hits rose by 2 000 while ours fell by 800, and by t=160 the army was gone. The replay names the difference in three numbers. Fire concentration: four or more of his shots on one target in 28 % of his firing ticks (five in six ticks), ours in 1 %. Target choice: he shot our ranged 120 times and our disarmed ranged 60 more (finishing them), we shot his melee 67 times and his disarmed melee 26 (his healers re-arm a melee in ten ticks; his melee adjacent to ours 54 % of their creep-ticks, ours 20 %). Silence: our ranged had a target in 213 creep-ticks and fired in 135, the 78 silent ones all disarmed — his focus took a ranged of ours apart every two ticks; his ranged were silent-disarmed in 9. Fire rate: his 369 shots at 75 % of creep-ticks, ours 150 at 40 %. Every one of these is the standing-line gap of the Coldkimchi losses (matches 53, 67, 73, 78, 80, 114) and of match 115, and the five placements measured against it (focus row, net tier, ranged front, press ring, fight-at-parity) were each rejected by the stub on outcomes; the stub's opponents do not concentrate fire, so it cannot see this. The detachments played no part (0 detach lines; the fight began at t=71). Left as the open item it is: a parity brawl is decided by the first exchange, and his first exchange puts four guns on one creep.

**Matches 141–142 (05.09.2026, the second series of v59): 1:1 — けろびー in 1 200 ticks (1150 -> 1159), then G1N6ERbreadMan, annihilated by t=400 after a rush into our home corner (1159 -> 1146). v60 — his armed ranged are the focus target before anything but a kill (the operator's call after match 140: 'focus his ranged first, as he does ours').** Match 142 had the shape of 140: EVADE from t=3 with the escape points trembling between home (85,88), the far H4 (8,90) and R3 (85,49) — five decisions in fifteen ticks (t=85–100), the home corner again leaving no direction — then contact at t=108 with our side at 3 967 against 4 087 (the scout had taken R3 at t=60 during the evade, −120 of power for 39 points), even at t=120 (11 682 against 10 794) and lost by t=180. His replay: 92 mass attacks, 1 477 adjacent heals, 73 shots into our disarmed melee to finish them, four or more shots on one target in 16 % of his firing ticks against our 9 %; with an armed ranged in reach we put 60 % of our shots into it and he 32 % (he was shooting our melee, 109 times). v60 adds one tier to the focus comparator right after 'killable this tick': an enemy with live ranged parts outranks every other target, and the sticky focus lets a melee go when an armed ranged enters reach (USE_FOCUS_RANGED_FIRST). The reasoning is match 140's arithmetic: the ranged are what kills (his 369 shots against our 150) and what comes apart fastest (600 hits of parts in front of an r6m6 against 800 in an a8m8), and his healers re-arm a disarmed melee in ten ticks. Gate 125 of 125, 22 worse / 19 better — the churn every fight rule makes (v52c 29/20, v53 29/30, v55 19/24), with 'destroyed' and 'lead' swapping both ways; the stub's opponents do not concentrate fire, so the live series is the measure. The detachments played no part in either match.

**Matches 143–145 (05.09.2026, the first series of v60): 2:1 — けろびー in 1 200 ticks (1146 -> 1153), G1N6ERbreadMan in 1 000 (-> 1155), then Coldkimchi a seventh time, the army erased in forty ticks at his 16 000 of 16 000 (1155 -> 1148). v61 — the fight plan's cells lie on our side of the line.** The G1N6ERbreadMan match was the rematch of 142 to the tick — the same evade around the home corner, contact at t=103 — and the fight went the other way in thirty-five ticks (his combat hits 16 000 -> 13 060 -> 7 144 -> 3 604 -> 0 from t=110 to 140, two of ours lost): with an armed ranged of his in reach our shooters put 35 of 35 shots into it (100 %, against 60 % in 142 and 52 % in 140), his ranged were silent-disarmed in 75 creep-ticks against our 9 (the mirror of match 140's 78 against 9), 124 shots to his 46. One sample of the ranged-first focus doing what it was written for. The Coldkimchi match had the shape of 78, 80 and 114: the evade east along the top edge, H4 in the corner at t≈130 (our 4 087 against his 4 191 after it), the post at (89,7) for a hundred and twenty ticks, then the escape points trembling across the whole map — (96,3) at t=248 with margin 11, (3,3) at 253 with −39, (12,9) at 278 with −25, two flee points, (12,9) again, (90,8) at 298 with −8 — the centroid (90,7) -> (73,4) -> (69,5) -> (73,4) -> (72,9) while his line came up from the south; contact at t=302, HOLD in contact at 0.97, and from t=363 the collapse: 11 694 of 14 400 at t=380, 5 168 at t=400, nothing at t=420, his side never scratched. The replay's fight window (360–399): his ranged within three of one of ours in 59 % of creep-ticks and firing 4.2 a tick, ours in 33 % firing 1.2; his melee at two or three from ours 72 % of the time (the poke); our ranged at four or more with a free cell ahead in 76 creep-ticks. The log names why the free cell stayed free: at t=370–380 every creep of ours had a fight-plan slot (so=0 flow=−1) and the slots were at (76–80, 15–16) — south of his line at (73–76, 9–13), with our centroid at (79,8) north of it. planFight's candidate cells are every cell within three of one of ours; its ranged tiers are 'a target within three, then no enemy melee adjacent, then none within two', and with his melee standing in front of his ranged the cells that satisfy all three are behind his line — so eleven creeps walked around his line one by one to reach them, under four guns each. v61 keeps a candidate cell only if it is no farther from the centroid of our armed creeps than from the centroid of his threats (USE_PLAN_OUR_SIDE). The ranged-first focus was not the cause here: only 1 of 3 ranged ever had a target in reach. Gate 125 of 125, 10 worse / 17 better against v60 (m33 farm+weak +12 127, m31 camp destroyed at 384 instead of 990, seven lines from lead to annihilation).

**Matches 146–150 (05.09.2026, the first series of v61): 5:1 — 欢欢 in 400 ticks, けろびー in 400, stachu3478 in 400, けろびー's farmer BEATEN for the first time after twelve losses, 23 586 : 22 525 in 1 900 ticks (1157 -> 1170), System in 1 700, then けろびー's fighting build again, the army gone by t=180 in the fight of match 140 (1170 -> 1161). v62 — the fight plan is for a standing line, not a melee brawl.** The farmer match is the v57–v59 chain working end to end: he never fired, the chase ran dry at t=482 and four ranged detached (five from t=618), the side parity let them capture, and they sat on D5, R3, R3 and H4 to the end — 15 a tick against his 10 from t=800, 18 against 7 from t=1800, the score crossing at t≈1780; not a shot either way. The last match had the opening of 140 and 150 to the tick (evade to (85,88), the corner (96,96) at −4, HOLD at home, contact at t=70 at 4 087 against 3 896) and lost the first ten ticks 1 670 hits to nothing. The log names it: at t=70 and t=80 every creep had a fight-plan slot — the armies' centres were not closing because his melee were already among ours, so v47's 'standing fight' fired — and the ranged slots were a column at x=81 one to three cells from his ranged; by t=80 two of our ranged stood north of his line on cells (77,71) and (78,71) that v61's half-plane does not cut, being equidistant from both centres, beside the fight rather than behind it. In a brawl the planner's tiers 'no enemy melee adjacent, none within two' point through his line. v62 requires for the standing fight that no armed melee of his is within MELEE_HOLD_RANGE + 1 of any of our armed — the condition the press signal already carries — so a brawl gets planBlock's rows behind the front melee, as v43b had it. The replay of the loss: our shots 100 % into an armed ranged in reach, his 62 %; his four-plus-on-one in 24 % of firing ticks, ours 0 %. Gate 125 of 125, 7 worse / 9 better against v61.

**Matches 151–152 (05.09.2026, the first series of v62): 1:1 — Hardy in 1 100 ticks (1161 -> 1162), then けろびー's camper a thirteenth time, 12 974 : 23 119 in 1 500 ticks (1162 -> 1160). v63 — an exception to the parity doctrine for a lost race against an opponent who has never fired, put to the operator.** The shape of match 133 with the detachments now working as written and still not enough: he parked his twelve on D5 — the vulnerability flag — from t=400 to the end; we held H4, A3 and R3 (10 a tick) and he D5, H4, A3 and R3 (15), his three other flags standing EMPTY the whole time. Two ranged detached at t=385 (the core's parity allowed no more against his 3 394); one sat on our H4, the other stood POISED a cell from his empty A3 at (31,66) for eleven hundred ticks. The reason is the parity floor itself, not the detachment: with our side at 3 618 against his 3 507, taking one flag lands at 0.88 (his relief counts too) against the floor of 0.97; the push wants 1.0 in a stalemate and we stood at 0.91; so the army HELD at (13,46) with `behind=true/762` and lost 10 to 15 a tick with certainty. The parity doctrine insures the side against annihilation. An opponent who has not fired once in fifteen hundred ticks — nor in any of thirteen matches — is not the threat it insures against, and a points loss by standing is the same loss. v63 lowers the capture floor to PARITY_FLOOR_LOST (0.75) when the race is lost (behind with the lower rate for BEHIND_PATIENCE, the existing stalemate signal) and he has never hit us (lastHurtTick == 0): from 3:4 flags at 0.91 two captures land at 0.77 by this match's numbers, and two are what the race needed (16 against 9 a tick from t=400). This is a change to the operator's doctrine made on evidence; it sits behind USE_LOST_RACE_CAPTURE and reverts with one line. The stub cannot see it — every stub farmer fires first. Gate 125 of 125, one line slower (m25 screen, destroyed at 627 instead of 365).

**Match 153 (05.09.2026, the first series of v63): 0:1 — Coldkimchi an eighth time, a seven-hundred-tick standing fight at our home corner lost without a kill (1160 -> 1153). v64 — a brawl is his melee adjacent, not within three.** His rush from t=3, contact at t=62, and then the longest standing fight of the day: even for three hundred ticks (15 242 against 14 720 at t=200, 14 838 against 14 319 at t=300), our melee gone between t=350 and 425, the ranged from t=700, nothing at t=800, his 16 000 of 16 000 at the end. The replay says we out-shot him — 1 611 shots at 52 % of ranged creep-ticks against his 1 428 at 40 %, a target within three 53 % against 40 % — and healed as much (1 511 adjacent heals against his 1 630), and lost everything because of where the shots went: four or more on one target in 9 of our 653 firing ticks against 44 of his 580 (7 %). At 216 healed a tick on a target, only four guns on one creep take anything off; two or three are healed back. His melee stood at two and three from ours the whole fight (2:699, 3:1431 creep-ticks, adjacent in 2 %, 61 swings in 700 ticks) — the poke of match 73, not a brawl — and v62's criterion 'his melee within MELEE_HOLD_RANGE + 1' read it as a brawl every tick, so the fight plan with its 'focus target within three' tier was off for the whole fight and planBlock's rows stood the ranged a row behind the front melee, each shooting what it reached. v64 defines the brawl as his armed melee ADJACENT to our armed (the existing theirMeleeIn), which match 150's brawl was (51 adjacent creep-ticks, 43 swings in fifty ticks) and this line was not. Gate 125 of 125, 6 worse / 6 better against v63. The lost-race exception of v63 did not come into play (a fight, not a race).

**Matches 154–158 (05.09.2026, the first series of v64): 4:1 — stachu3478 in 400 ticks, けろびー's fighting build in 1 200 and in 1 900 (his army destroyed by t=400, all seven flags ours to the end, 1160 -> 1173, the day's high), あぶらむし in 1 100, then けろびー's farmer a fourteenth time, 5 402 : 23 167 in 1 200 ticks (1173 -> 1163). v65 — the farmer is the one who has been quiet for FARMER_QUIET ticks, not the one who never fired.** Five detached at t=157 (never hit, in reach, the chase dry), and at t=254–259 his blob walked into the core standing on our R3 at (13,49): a real exchange, his melee there taken from 1 600 to 16 hits and a ranged to two parts, one of ours to M8A3 — then he left and never fired again. That one exchange set lastHurtTick and switched off both the persistent detachment (v57) and the lost-race capture floor (v63) for the remaining 940 ticks: the army pushed at two to one (4 179 against 1 981, three of one at t=1100 after killing four of his), never caught the rest, and he farmed 18–22 a tick against our 3–9. 'Never hit' was written against the stub's kiter, which bites with its bait at t=76–84 and comes back for a split core at t≈246 — 160 ticks later. This farmer's one exchange was followed by 940 quiet ticks. v65 puts the farmer's silence at FARMER_QUIET = PASSIVE_TICKS × 3 (300 ticks since the last hit, or never) in both places; what would make it wrong is a kiter that waits three hundred ticks before coming back. Gate 125 of 125, identical to v64 on every line — the stub's kiter bites in the first hundred ticks and returns before three hundred.

**Match 159 (05.09.2026, the first series of v65): 0:1 — けろびー's farmer a fifteenth time, 12 009 : 24 099 in 1 500 ticks (1163 -> 1161). v66 — an army standing on its own flag holds that flag.** No detachment fired: his armed centroid stayed on D5 whenever we were away and moved only when we came — the detach signal wants him to leave under a chase, and he does not leave, he steps aside. What the log shows instead is match 70's finding in its purest form: the army at 4 075 against his 3 084 flickered ANNIHILATE (pushing, huntable 12) with HOLD (huntable 0) and every HOLD sent it to the post at (11,68) or (17,68), forty cells from D5; our centroid went (11,67) -> (36,59) -> (56,46) -> (56,69) -> (46,49) -> (53,61) -> (46,51) -> (37,55) -> (11,67) over t=600–1400 while his sat on D5 at (48,49) at every sample but the ones where we were on it. Twice we were: D5 ours at t=800, our melee standing on (49,49) at t=1000 with the keeper log naming it (kept at t=1009, released at t=1011 because his nine armed within ten cells are not a picket), and each time the next HOLD walked the army back to the corner and he retook the flag. v66: with the enemy near, an army whose centroid is within POST_STANDOFF of a flag of ours takes that flag as its post — hold what you hold — instead of the intercept post or the post point. 'Hold the line where it stands' in general was measured and rejected in v42 (58 worse / 48 better, the walk back to the post finishing repelled rushes); this is only the flag underfoot. Gate 125 of 125, 0 worse / 2 better against v65 (m32 farm+weak from lead to annihilation at 1683, m33 farm+weak +3 349).

**Match 160 (05.09.2026, the first series of v66): 0:1 — Coldkimchi a ninth time, the army gone by t=560 at his 16 000 of 16 000 (1161 -> 1154). v67 — a fight-plan cell's targets are his ranged.** The shape of 78, 80, 114 and 145: the evade east along the top edge, H4 in the corner at t≈100, the post at (86–92, 4–6) for a hundred and sixty ticks, his line up the right edge, contact at t=313, a break, and the fight at (65–75, 25–32) from t=440 to 560. Our target choice was perfect and our reach was not: 85 of 85 shots into an armed ranged when one was within three, but only 85 such shots, and 79 more into his melee 'for want of a ranged' — his ranged stood at three and four from ours (3:198, 4:213 creep-ticks), his melee at two and three (74 %), and our ranged spent 142 creep-ticks at four or more with a free cell ahead. The plan slots at t=470–490 are why: every creep had one, and the planner's 'a target within three' tier counted his poking melee as the target, so a cell three from his melee and four from his ranged qualified and the placement memory kept it. His fire 341 shots to our 199, four-plus on one target in 10 % of his firing ticks against 0 % of ours. v67 counts a cell's targets among his armed RANGED (the focus doctrine of v60 applied to placement), with his melee counting only when he has no ranged left. Gate 125 of 125, 11 worse / 13 better against v66 — the churn of a fight rule, 'destroyed' and 'lead' swapping both ways.

**Match 161 (05.09.2026, the first series of v67): 0:1 — けろびー's farmer a sixteenth time, 19 771 : 23 843 in 1 800 ticks (1154 -> 1152). v68 — the detachment forms on a lost race with a quiet opponent, not only on a chase that runs dry.** The chain worked and came late: the detachment fired at t=806 (four, then five), and from t=900 to the end we held D5, both A3 and R3 against his two H4 and R3 — 14 a tick against 11 for nine hundred ticks — and closed the gap from 6 450 to 4 072 without reaching him. The first eight hundred ticks were the doctrine's own oscillation: push and take flags (D5, H4, A3, A3 by t=400), be weaker for the debuffs (3 234 against 3 885), EVADE, lose the flags to his walk-over, be stronger again (4 087 against 3 070), push — and only that push, running dry at t=806, let the detachment form. He never fired. v68 adds a second entry: the opponent quiet for FARMER_QUIET since his FIRST reach (so a rusher at t=100 is not a farmer) and the race lost (behind with the lower rate) — in this match t≈400 instead of 806, fourteen hundred ticks at 14 against 11 for a deficit of 4 500. On the stub the second entry fires only in the two farm+weak lines (five or six detached at t=411–545 after three hundred quiet ticks) and both go from 'destroyed' to 'lead' — the stub's remnant is destroyable and the detachment lets it live, the live farmer is not and never was. Gate 125 of 125, 2 worse / 0 better against v67. The second series of v67 (matches 162–163) went 2:1: stachu3478 in 100 ticks, けろびー's fighting build destroyed by t=600 in 1 400 (-> 1163), then that build again in 200 (-> 1152) — the top-edge brawl of matches 140 and 150 once more, even to t=100 (9 501 against 8 244), his re-arm from t=110, our army gone by t=160.

**Matches 164–169 (06.09.2026, the first series of v68): 5:1 — G1N6ERbreadMan twice in 1 100 ticks, 欢欢 in 1 100, Hardy in 1 000, けろびー in 1 100 (1152 -> 1163), then stachu3478 for the third time in twenty-seven meetings, the army gone by t=220 at his 15 435 of 16 000 (1163 -> 1150). v69 — a ranged creep's plan cell is never ahead of the melee front.** The fight at our home corner from t=70 was even to t=120 (14 114 against 15 075, five of five ranged with a target in reach) and then his line backed off a cell a tick — his centroid (78,79) -> (69,70) -> (61,61) -> (57,57) — and the army followed it apart: v67's cells 'with his ranged within three' moved with his line, our ranged walked into them, and the melee, whose cells the placement memory kept, stayed eight to ten cells behind (t=130: ranged at (64–71, 61–66), melee at (72–74, 67–71)); his melee, at three of ours in 41 % of their creep-ticks, took the ranged apart one by one (19 silent-disarmed creep-ticks, 30 of his shots into our armed ranged and 16 into the disarmed), reach 0 of 4 at t=140, 12 -> 11 -> 9 -> 6 -> 2 -> 0 creeps from t=130 to 220. Our target choice was again perfect (60 of 60 shots into an armed ranged in reach) and irrelevant. 'The ranged in front' was rejected as a formation in v37; v67 let it in through the plan. v69 keeps a ranged cell no closer to the nearest threat than the current melee front minus one step (USE_RANGED_BEHIND_MELEE), the fallback being any cell. Gate 125 of 125, 7 worse / 6 better against v68.

**Matches 170–173 (06.09.2026, the first series of v69): 3:1 — あぶらむし in 1 700 ticks, 欢欢 in 1 100, けろびー in 300 (1150 -> 1154), then けろびー's fighting build, the army gone by t=260 at his 16 000 of 16 000 (1154 -> 1148). v70 — the focus target is the one most of our guns reach.** He took flags first and came at the corner post (87,8) at 3 630 against our 4 191; the push met him at (79,16) and the exchange was even for twenty ticks (14 282 against 15 114 at t=200, 11 168 against 12 179 at t=220) — then twelve creeps became four in twenty ticks. The replay: four or more of his shots on one target in 13 % of his firing ticks, ours in 0 %; our ranged silent-disarmed 48 creep-ticks, his 0; at t=220 four of our five ranged were down to two or three parts or none and the healers to one, and the log shows the whole army rotating toward the last healer. Our target choice was perfect again (57 of 57 shots into an armed ranged in reach) and it did not matter: his five shoot our most forward creep — the one all five reach by construction — while our focus takes his ranged by threat and kill-time, which one or two of ours reach. v70 adds a tier right after 'ranged first': among equals, the target more of our ranged reach (gunsAt), and the sticky focus yields to a target that FOCUS_GUNS_SWITCH (2) more guns reach. Concentration is a target we can reach, not a target we want.
